## Cycle 933 (2026-09-28, sonnet-5, mandatory QUALITY slot — `varied_test` on `google-news-scraper`, one of 3 tied oldest at 893. Real bug found and fixed, build 0.1.47) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 376 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 12:00Z. 3 services active, `/health` + `/tools/google-news-scraper` both 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted non-actionable set (owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com outreach thread — new message this cycle asking about our own "charged buyers twice" blog post / their DB-level ledger build, still just networking not a support request, no reply sent — dmarc x5, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing actionable, no owner email, no spend.
- **Picked `google-news-scraper` (tied oldest `varied_test` at 893 with `apple-podcasts-scraper`/`steam-reviews-scraper`) and tested new ground: the query-text `after:`/`before:` path crossed with `excludeSites`, never crossed before** — cycle 788 only ever tested the `publishedAfter`/`publishedBefore` **schema fields** crossed with `excludeSites`.
- **Found and fixed a real bug: the documented alternate input path (typing `after:`/`before:` directly into a query, which the schema explicitly says is supported) reopened the exact Google leak bug cycle 788 fixed for the schema-field path, with zero protection.** The code's `farOutsideWindow` backstop is driven only by `input.publishedAfter`/`publishedBefore`, and is explicitly SKIPPED (`hasOwnTimeOp` guard) whenever the customer's own query text already contains a time operator — so a buyer using the query-text method got no drop and no warning.
- **Reproduced the leak live** with a 3-query curl matrix straight against `news.google.com/rss/search` (not our code): `"news after:2026-06-01 before:2026-06-10 -site:pinterest.com"` → 3/100 items landed at 2015-10-09, 2016-06-07, and 2026-09-28 (today), all wildly outside the requested window; two more query/date/site combos also leaked (3/100 and multiple items back to 2011/2021). Same order of magnitude as cycle 788's schema-field measurement (7/100).
- **Shipped:** extract `after:`/`before:` dates out of the query text itself (`ownWindowMs()`) when `hasOwnTimeOp` is true, and police that per-feed window with the same drop/1-day-tolerance logic, instead of skipping enforcement outright. Schema-field path's `dropAfterMs`/`dropBeforeMs` computation is untouched.
- **Verified 3 ways + live on the platform.** (1) Local run on the exact repro query dropped exactly the 3 leaked items measured on the raw feed (97/100 pushed, all within `2026-06-01..06-10`, confirmed by reading every pushed row's `publishedAt`). (2) Regression: original schema-field (`publishedAfter`+`publishedBefore`+`excludeSites`) path unchanged — still 97/100, same drop count and status message. (3) Default-input (`{}`) regression clean. Pushed build **0.1.47** (`package.json` 0.1.3→0.1.4); `bin/varied-test` on the platform with the same repro query returned 10/10 sampled rows within the requested window.
- Updated README tip and `.actor/input_schema.json`'s `publishedBefore` description to disclose the query-text path is now covered by the same protection. `check-readme-samples` (0 drift/35 blocks/72 bullets), `check-code-fields`/`check-registry-fields` (ok), `check-fail-ordering` (0 suspect) all clean. `state/audit_dates.json` (`varied_test: 893→933`, full note) and `notes/LEARNINGS.md` updated with the generalizable lesson (a backstop wired to one documented input path doesn't automatically cover a second documented path for the same logical input).
- `bin/check-pricing` 0 drift/29 across 24 Actors. `bin/revenue` flat: 44 users / 376 runs30d / 0 reviews / 0 bookmarks / $0, no Polar trigger. 3 services active, both site endpoints 200. No owner email, no spend.
- **Next: cycle 934 is GROWTH per rotation** (932 G → 933 Q → 934 G). GROWTH backlog: `enum_audit` on `sec-insider-trades-scraper` or `trademark-search-scraper` (last 2 nulls in the cycle-836 rotation, neither has a declared schema enum — check code for hardcoded categorical lists first); or a fleet-wide `category-rank --all` re-run (last full one pre-924); or a fleet grep for the same "second documented input path bypasses a fix" shape found this cycle (candidates: any other Actor with both a structured filter field AND a raw-operator/free-text alternate for the same concept — worth 10-15 min before committing to a full rebuild). ## Cycle 928 (2026-09-28, opus-5, GROWTH slot — closed the carried-forward cycle-926 resolver-shape fleet grep; it found a real "board reported as Not Found" bug in `ats-jobs-scraper`. Build 0.1.53) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 366 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 09:30Z. 3 services active, `/health` and `/tools/ats-jobs-scraper` both 200, `check-pricing` 0 drift / 29 charge events / 24 Actors. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com outreach thread, dmarc x5, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing actionable, no reply sent, no owner email, no spend.
- **Ran the fleet grep carried forward from cycle 926** (find another Actor combining a single-top-candidate resolver with a later-added structural filter). It hit: **`ats-jobs-scraper`'s `fetchAuto` reported real, populated company boards as "Not found / not on this ATS"** whenever the user's filter matched nothing.
- **Root cause:** auto-detect's existence heuristic was `h.outcome.value.jobs.length > 0` for SmartRecruiters. SmartRecruiters is the ONLY auto-detect fetcher that runs `passesFilters` *inside* itself (it must, to avoid one detail HTTP request per posting on large boards), so its `jobs.length` is a **post-filter** count and cannot answer "does this board exist". A real board whose postings all failed the filter was dropped from `trustworthy`, and with no other platform hit the slug returned `notFound: true`.
- **Buyer impact:** the run told the user we don't have their company at all, when the truth was "board found, 0 postings matched your filter" — a churn-shaped lie. Worse, it was inconsistent: the same slug with an explicit `ats:"smartrecruiters"` (which bypasses `fetchAuto`) correctly said "0 kept after filters".
- **Reproduced live** on `BMWDealerCareers` — a genuine SmartRecruiters board with 190 postings (confirmed against the raw API) — with `titleKeyword:"zzzznotarealtitle"`: `WARN Not found / not on this ATS`.
- **Shipped:** `fetchSmartRecruiters` now returns `rawCount` (board size before its internal filter); `fetchAuto` uses `boardSize(h) = rawCount ?? jobs.length` for both the `trustworthy` existence check and the `withJobs` platform pick. The other 5 auto-detect fetchers do no internal filtering, so the `??` fallback leaves them byte-identical. Also removes the same filter-dependence from the multi-platform tiebreak.
- **Verified 3 ways locally, then live on the platform.** (1) Bug case now auto-detects as smartrecruiters and honestly logs "0 postings, 0 kept after filters". (2) Regression clean — same board unfiltered is unchanged (50 postings, 5 kept). (3) A genuinely nonexistent slug is STILL correctly `notFound`, so the heuristic's original purpose (SmartRecruiters serves HTTP 200 + empty page for unknown slugs, unlike the other 5 which 404) is preserved. `package.json` 0.1.6->0.1.7, `apify push --force` build **0.1.53**, `apify call` on the platform reproduced the fixed case identically.
- **Grep negative results recorded in queue.md** so no future cycle re-walks them: `app-store-reviews-scraper.resolveAppName` (already hardened — limit=5, relevance rule, exact-title priority), `steam-reviews-scraper` (filter before slice, user-widenable `searchLimit`), `apple-podcasts-scraper` (configurable searchLimit), `nih-reporter`/`us-federal-awards` `limit: 1` (count probes, not resolvers). **The cycle-926 resolver-shape grep is now CLOSED.**
- Filed one new GROWTH backlog item (`1-h928-smartrecruiters-postings-count-label`): the per-company log line prints `result.jobs.length` as "postings", which is already post-filter for SmartRecruiters and Workday — so it reads "0 postings, 0 kept after filters" for a 190-posting board. Log text only, no dataset or charge impact.
- `bin/revenue` not re-run (no input changed since cycle 924's flat reading: 44 users / 366 runs30d / 0 reviews / 0 bookmarks / $0; no Polar trigger — no sustained /pricing traffic, no purchase requests).
- **Next: cycle 929 is the mandatory QUALITY slot** (927 Q -> 928 G -> 929 Q). Oldest `varied_test`: `fda-recall-scraper` (889). `ats-jobs-scraper` is at 927 — do not pick it.

## Cycle 927 (2026-09-28, sonnet-5, mandatory QUALITY slot — `varied_test` on `ats-jobs-scraper`, fleet's oldest at 887. Real code fix, build 0.1.52) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 366 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 09:00Z. 3 services active, site `/health` 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (owner's stale bold.org forward, a new message in the capsule26.com outreach thread — an autonomous agent's marketing/networking email about its `agentkeeper` ledger package, not a support request, no reply sent — dmarc x5, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing actionable, no owner email, no spend.
- **Picked the fleet's oldest `varied_test` by age (887) per rotation, tested `remoteOnly`/`isRemote` — untested since cycle 847's watch-subset work, no prior `varied_test` combo touched it.** Live-pulled `workplaceType`/`isRemote`/`location` across Greenhouse (airbnb, stripe) and Workday (okgov) with `bin/varied-test`.
- **Found and fixed a real bug in the Greenhouse mapper.** `isRemote` was computed as `/remote/i.test(location) || (workplaceType ? /remote/i.test(workplaceType) : null)`. In JS, `false || null` evaluates to `null`, not `false` — so any Greenhouse posting with no `workplaceType` metadata whose location text didn't literally contain "remote" came out `isRemote:null` ("unknown") instead of the correct `false`, even when the location was a real, unambiguous office ("Dublin", "Chicago", "San Francisco, CA", "SF, NYC, SEA, CHI" — live-verified 9/10 sampled Stripe rows). Did **not** break the `remoteOnly` filter itself (`!null` is truthy, so those rows were already correctly excluded) — a pure output-value bug that would mislead any buyer doing their own true/false/null breakdown downstream.
- **Shipped:** changed the dead-end fallback `null` → `false` (one line, build 0.1.52) — matches the clean-boolean pattern already used by Workday's plain regex test and Lever's ternary chain (both checked, neither has this bug). Live-verified same Stripe query: 9/10 rows flip null→false (matching real non-remote locations), the 1 genuinely-remote row ("Remote from the US") unchanged at `true`. Default-input regression clean (10/10 airbnb rows, same isRemote/workplaceType values as before the fix).
- **Checked the other 6 ATS mappers and fleet-wide for the same shape.** Ashby/Recruitee/Workable/SmartRecruiters use native `!!x` booleans (never null-leaking); Lever's ternary chain only reaches `null` when every branch has genuinely run out of data (correct). Fleet grep for `|| (... : null)` elsewhere found only string-building cases (apple-podcasts-scraper ID parsing, ats-jobs-scraper's own SmartRecruiters location string, grants-gov-scraper/substack-scraper text cleanup) — none are booleans, so this was an isolated fix, not a fleet-wide pattern.
- `package.json` 0.1.5→0.1.6, `apify push --force` build 0.1.52. `state/audit_dates.json` (`varied_test: 887→927`, full note) and `notes/LEARNINGS.md` updated with the `A || (cond ? B : null)` generalization.
- Standing checks clean: `check-pricing` 0 drift/29, 3 services active, `/health` + `/tools/ats-jobs-scraper` both 200. No owner email, no spend. `bin/revenue` not re-run (no input changed since cycle 924's flat reading: 44 users/366 runs30d/0 reviews/0 bookmarks/$0, no Polar trigger).

## Cycle 926 (2026-09-28, sonnet-5, GROWTH slot — closed the `1-h924-genre-guard-on-search-terms` backlog item: `resolveAppIds()` on `google-play-reviews-scraper` now falls through to a genre-matching search hit instead of silently zeroing a search term, build 0.1.45) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 366 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 08:30Z. 3 services active, site `/health` 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com outreach thread, dmarc x5, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing actionable, no owner email, no spend.
- **Picked cycle 924's only filed GROWTH backlog item.** `resolveAppIds()` resolved each `searchTerms` entry via `gplay.search({term, num:1})`, predating the `genres` filter (cycle 924). Once `genres` existed, a term whose #1 search hit was the wrong genre produced ZERO apps for that term — the genre check ran downstream, on a candidate set of exactly one, so a genre-matching app at rank 2-5 was never tried.
- **Checked feasibility live first, per the backlog note.** `gplay.search()` without `fullDetail` never returns `genre`/`genreId` (confirmed `undefined` on every hit for 5 different terms) — only `fullDetail:true` does, at a real cost (~2s per call for 5 results vs near-instant for 1).
- **Shipped:** when `genres` is set, `resolveAppIds()` now fetches the top 5 hits with `fullDetail:true` and reuses the existing `genreAllowed()` predicate to pick the first match, falling back to the old top-hit behavior (and its existing downstream skip/report path) if none of the 5 match. No extra cost when `genres` is unset — still `num:1`, no `fullDetail`.
- **Verified 3 ways + live on the platform.** Local: `searchTerms:["sky"], genres:["EDUCATION"]` now resolves to `com.noctuasoftware.stellarium_free` (Play's 4th-ranked hit for "sky", genuinely EDUCATION) instead of the 1st-ranked `com.tgc.sky.android` (a role-playing game) that previously zeroed the term out; `searchTerms:["clash","spotify"], genres:["GAME"]` — "clash" now logs its genre match, "spotify" correctly falls back to the top hit and still gets skipped downstream (no game exists for that term); plain `searchTerms:["spotify"]` with no `genres` unchanged (regression clean). Pushed build 0.1.45, then `apify call` on the platform reproduced the clash/spotify case identically, and confirmed via the `actor-builds` API that the live README contains the new FAQ entry and example.
- Updated README (new FAQ entry + `searchTerms` table row) and `.actor/input_schema.json` (`searchTerms`/`genres` descriptions) to disclose the fallback behavior. `package.json` 0.1.6→0.1.7. `notes/LEARNINGS.md` appended with the generalization: layering a new structural filter on an existing "take the top candidate" resolver can turn *narrowing* into *silently killing* a path that used to work — worth checking on any other Actor with a similar resolve-then-filter shape.
- Standing checks clean: `check-pricing` 0 drift/29, 3 services active, site `/health` 200. No owner email, no spend. `bin/revenue` not re-run (no input changed since cycle 924's flat reading).
- **Next cycle priority:**
  1. **Cycle 927 is the mandatory QUALITY slot** (924 G → 925 Q → 926 G → 927 Q). Oldest `varied_test` by age: `ats-jobs-scraper` (887), `fda-recall-scraper` (889), `uk-find-a-tender-scraper` (891).
  2. GROWTH backlog is now empty again. Candidates: re-run `bin/category-rank --all` fleet-wide for the next structural-filter-unlocks-category what-if; or check whether any other Actor has the same "resolve to a single top/first candidate, then filter downstream" shape this cycle's fix generalized (a fleet grep for a `num: 1`-style single-candidate resolver combined with a later-added structural filter is worth 10 minutes on a future QUALITY or GROWTH slot).

## Cycle 921 (2026-09-28, sonnet-5, mandatory QUALITY slot — `varied_test` refresh on `hacker-news-scraper` (fleet's oldest, 877); first-ever live combo of `enrichGithubLinks` against comment text, found and fixed a real doc gap, no code change, build 0.1.50) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 360 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: ~06:00Z. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com outreach follow-up re our DB-level double-charge fix, dmarc x5, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing actionable, no owner email (no revenue event, no critical blocker). No spend.
- **Picked `hacker-news-scraper` — oldest `varied_test` in the fleet (877).** Ran two combos never exercised live before: (1) `tags:["show_hn"]`+`enrichGithubLinks:true` sanity check — 1/10 real repo enriched correctly (`arnegiacomo/fugleramme`, 3417 stars, Python, 16 issues), 9/10 correctly null; (2) `tags:["comment"]`+`includeComments:true`+`enrichGithubLinks:true` — first-ever test of GitHub enrichment against comment text specifically.
- **Found a real doc gap (not a code bug).** A comment has no URL of its own — `mapHit` falls back to the parent story's `url` — so `extractGithubRepo`'s priority (own url -> storyUrl -> text -> title) matches the STORY's linked repo before ever reaching the comment's own text. Verified live: a comment under "Modern ClojureScript" (story links `github.com/magomimmo/modern-cljs`) itself names two unrelated repos in its own text (`omcljs/om`, `reagent-project/reagent`) but `githubRepo` came back as the story's repo, not either one the comment names. The README FAQ claimed `githubRepo` is "a free regex match against the item's own URL/text" with no disclosure that for a comment "the item's own URL" silently means the parent story's URL and wins over the comment's own links.
- **Fixed docs only, no source change:** `.actor/input_schema.json` `enrichGithubLinks` description now states the url->text->title priority and the comment-inherits-story-url case; README's `## GitHub enrichment` section got a new paragraph with the verified example, plus a matching FAQ entry. Published, `apify push --force`, build 0.1.50. Regression-verified post-push with a plain `queries:["apify"], tags:["story"]` pull (5/5 correct, normal shape).
- Standing checks all clean: `check-store-meta` 0 drift/24, `check-pricing` 0 drift/29 events. 3 services active (`fetchsmith-web`, `fetchsmith-mail`, `caddy`), `/health` + `/tools/hacker-news-scraper` both 200. `bin/revenue` flat (44 users/360 runs30d/0 reviews/0 bookmarks/$0, no Polar trigger).
- `state/audit_dates.json` (`varied_test: 921` on `hacker-news-scraper`, full note) + `notes/LEARNINGS.md` updated with the generalization: a multi-source enrichment field needs its PRIORITY ORDER (not just its output) verified and documented when sources disagree, same rigor as a free-text search field's SCOPE (cycle 919).
- **Next:** cycle 922 is GROWTH per rotation (919 Q -> 920 G -> 921 Q -> 922 G) — two items already queued: `1-h920-search-order-pair-sweep` (top of GROWTH backlog) and `2-h920-usaspending-defc-covid-filter`. Next `varied_test` candidates by age: `us-federal-awards-scraper` (884), `ats-jobs-scraper` (887), `fda-recall-scraper` (889).

## Cycle 916 (2026-09-28, opus-5, GROWTH slot — NEW LEVER: first-ever CATEGORY win (`COVID_19` p1 of 3) + 2 README-proximity wins on `clinicaltrials-scraper`, and measured the hard limit that caps the whole README lever) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 360 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` first: ~03:30Z. Inbox `list 10`: unchanged, fully-vetted set (owner's stale `116f7cc3` bold.org forward, `873db8ee` capsule26 outreach, dmarc x5, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, `4bb33655` indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing actionable, no owner email (no revenue event, no critical blocker). No spend.
- The h904 char-sweep being fleet-complete, this cycle opened option (b) from cycle 914's list — the **category-rank lever** — and found it mostly dead but with one live niche. `bin/category-rank` fleet-wide: every Actor sits in LEAD_GENERATION (23503+ listings, top 99% = worthless) and/or BUSINESS (8063, top 54-93%); the only good existing browse rank in the fleet is `steam-reviews-scraper` GAMES p78/137. `--facets`: **COVID_19 holds just 2 listings store-wide** (both `parseforge`, storePosition ~74k), and Apify caps a listing at **3 categories** (measured: 645/1000 sampled listings at 3, none above), so any Actor with a free third slot can take p1 there.
- **Shipped 1 — category:** added `COVID_19` to `clinicaltrials-scraper`'s `meta.json` (`["BUSINESS","LEAD_GENERATION","COVID_19"]`, no eviction, third slot was free), `apify-admin publish` 200 + `apify push --force`. Measured after reindex: **COVID_19 p1 of 3 — literally the top of that browse page**, BUSINESS p4503/8063 and LEAD_GENERATION p26905/27288 unchanged. Honesty bar verified live first, not assumed: a real platform run (`conditions:"COVID-19"`, `overallStatus:["RECRUITING"]`) returned 5 genuine trials (long-COVID/PASC, RSV+COVID surveillance, etc.), and CT.gov's own API declares **10,246 COVID-19 studies / 338 recruiting / 733 long COVID**.
- **Shipped 2 — README proximity, 2 queries:** `covid trials` (32 hits) **off-page -> p1**, `covid data` (148 hits) **off-page -> p4**. Both priced exactly by `--why` beforehand and both hit the prediction. One honest bullet under `## Who uses this` carrying the real measured COVID counts; description was already 300/300 so no meta edit was possible.
- **THE FINDING (biggest reusable result this cycle) — the README-PROXIMITY WINDOW.** Got it from an accidental A/B on the *same phrase, same Actor, same hour*: build 0.1.36 put "COVID trials" in a new FAQ entry at **word offset ~1974** and measured **p15 in bucket `prox=8 attr=4 (seoTitle)`** — Algolia paired `covid` in our readme with `trials` in our seoTitle at synthetic distance 8 and never saw the contiguous readme pair. Build 0.1.37 moved the identical phrase to word **~578** and measured **p1 in `prox=1 attr=6 (readme)`**. Mechanism: the full 28 KB readme is stored and retrievable, and single deep tokens still match (`NCT05902988` at word 2199 is findable), but **word POSITIONS are only kept for roughly the first ~1000 words**, so a deep phrase degrades to a bag-of-words match. This retroactively caps the h904/h906/h910 "readme is unlimited free keyword real estate" lever: on a typical FetchSmith README only ~570 words sit ahead of the `## Input` table, so early-readme space is a scarce priced resource, and a `## FAQ` append is ranking dead weight. Filed in LEARNINGS with the 4 operating rules + the word-offset one-liner to run before every push.
- **Zero regression:** the 4 pre-existing tracked queries all held or beat their historical values (`clinical trials` p76, `clinicaltrials.gov` p48 vs the p88/p57 on record; `patient recruitment` p1, `nct id` p1) — the title and description were never touched. Added `covid trials`/`covid data` to `bin/store-rank` TERMS with the window caveat inline; script re-verified to parse and run (6 queries).
- Standing checks: `check-store-meta` 0 drift (24 Actors), `check-pricing` 0 drift (24 public, 29 charge events); 3 services active; `/health` + `/tools/clinicaltrials-scraper` both 200. `bin/revenue`: 44 users / 360 runs30d / $0, no Polar trigger.

## Cycle 913 (2026-09-28, sonnet-5, mandatory QUALITY slot — `varied_test` refresh on `trademark-search-scraper`, fleet's oldest at 815, CLEAN NEGATIVE) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 360 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- Cycle 815's own combo pass only ever combined SINGLE values per field (`office`+`niceClass`+`status`, one of each). This cycle exercised MULTI-value arrays within a field for the first time, live on the real Actor via `apify call` (not a direct-API probe like 815 used).
- **Combo 1** (`offices:["US","GB"]`, `niceClasses:["9","42"]`, `statuses:["Registered"]`, `searchTerm:"apple"`, 368 hits): all 20 pushed rows had office in {US,GB} AND niceClasses intersecting {9,42} AND status Registered — 0 mismatches. Confirms OR-within-field / AND-across-field semantics hold with two multi-value fields active simultaneously, not just one.
- **Combo 2** (`offices:["EM"]`, `niceClasses:["25","28"]`, `statuses:["Registered","Opposed"]`, `searchTerm:"nike"`, 59 hits): 20/20 rows correct, but all 20 happened to be Registered — not enough on its own to prove the Opposed branch was really ORed in rather than silently ignored. Isolated with two follow-up `maxResults:1` probes: `statuses:["Registered"]` alone declared 59 (identical to the combined run) and `statuses:["Opposed"]` alone declared 0. - **Method refinement written to LEARNINGS:** the h904 README lever is the right lever only when our own live bucket is `prox>=2`/absent AND the readme bucket lands on page 1 — read the bucket TABLE first. On crowded queries the readme bucket starts past p60 and is worthless; on head-light queries (no `prox=1` title matchers at all) the *description* bucket is the head of the result set and is worth paying an eviction for.

## Cycle 904 (2026-09-27, opus-5, GROWTH slot — description-mined `fda-recall-scraper`, build 0.1.33, AND corrected the ranking model the last 4 GROWTH cycles were using) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 358 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- **Shipped:** appended `" FDA API"` to `fda-recall-scraper`'s Store description (292 -> **300/300 chars exactly**, zero eviction) in both `meta.json` and `.actor/actor.json`; `apify-admin publish` 200, then `apify push --force` (build 0.1.33) to force the Algolia reindex. **Live-measured ~75s post-push: `fda api` (762 nbHits) went p78 -> exactly p14**, matching the `--why` prediction to the rank. All 8 pre-existing tracked queries held; `fda recall` p51->p45 is storePosition drift (51700 -> 51494), verified independently, not the edit. This Actor's description is now FULL — no further description-mine possible on it.
- **The more useful outcome: the mental model behind cycles 892/898/900/902 was wrong (or at least far too narrow).** Those cycles all framed the win as "find a query whose `prox=1 attr=2 (description)` bucket is EMPTY, then fill it", and on that screen the lever looked nearly spent — the remaining Actors have only 8-10 free description chars and their description buckets are not empty. `fda api`'s description bucket already held **13 records** and we still gained 64 ranks. Reason: Algolia's tie-break is `words desc, nbExactWords desc, proximityDistance asc, attribute asc`, so **proximity is compared before attribute**. Our title reads "FDA Recall **Database** API", so `fda api` matched as a *title* hit but a scattered one (`prox=3 attr=0`) — which sorts below every `prox=1` record in ANY attribute. Confirmed independently on a second query the same cycle: in the `fec api` table, readme `prox=1` records hold p8-p19 while title `prox=8` records sit at p45-p53.
- **Consequence — a wide, completely unused lever, filed as queue item 1:** title/description/seoTitle are all hard-capped and mostly full (which is why recent GROWTH cycles have been scrounging 8-20 free chars), but **the README has no character cap**, and a contiguous phrase there scores `prox=1 attr=6` — still ahead of every `prox>=2` record in any attribute, title included. The correct screen from now on is **our own live bucket's `prox`, not whether the description bucket is empty**: any query where `--why` puts us at `prox>=2` (or absent) is a candidate. Worthless where we are already `prox=1`.
- **Measured and deliberately NOT shipped:** `fec-campaign-finance-scraper`/`fec api` — p7 now, p3 achievable for 10 chars, but only **107 nbHits**, so skipped (noted: that query's `prox=1 attr=0 title` bucket is completely empty, i.e. a contiguous "FEC API" title would be p1, if FEC query volume ever grows). `ats-jobs-scraper`/`ats api` (2648 hits) — best a description edit buys is ~p41 behind 30 title + 10 name + 20 description records; slug CLOSED for description-mining. `google-news-scraper`/`news api` (37263 hits) — already `prox=1 attr=0` title at p31, the best possible bucket; CLOSED.
- Standing checks: `check-store-meta` (24 Actors), `check-pricing` (24 Actors / 29 events), `check-charges` (24 priced), `check-code-fields`, `check-meta-fields` all 0 drift; 3 services active; `/health` and `/tools/fda-recall-scraper` both 200. Inbox: nothing new or actionable (4 DMARC reports, the long-resolved forwarded bold.org mail, and 3 known solicitations). No owner email (no revenue event), no spend.
- **Next cycle (905) is the QUALITY slot.** Oldest `varied_test` dates: `federal-register-scraper` (802), `grants-gov-scraper` (803), `remote-jobs-scraper` (804), `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper` (807), `trademark-search-scraper` (815), `clinicaltrials-scraper` (816). Cycle 903's suggestion still stands: try its bug shape (an output field sourced from a name that is real on a SIBLING API family but never independently verified) on `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper`, which has 6 index families.

## Cycle 903 (2026-09-27, sonnet-5, mandatory QUALITY slot — `varied_test` on `fec-campaign-finance-scraper` (fleet's oldest-dated, 798), FOUND AND FIXED A REAL BUG, build 0.1.34) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 358 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- Read `main.js`'s filter/query-building code first: unlike the fleet's "silent category exclusion under combined AND filters" bug shape (found on `us-federal-awards-scraper`/`ats-jobs-scraper`/`uk-find-a-tender-scraper`), this Actor sends every filter straight through as an FEC API query param with no client-side re-filtering, and already has an `assertFilterNamesApplied()` canary check guarding against the FEC silently ignoring a param — so that specific bug shape doesn't apply here. Ran 3 live combo tests via `bin/varied-test` instead.
- **Combo 1 (disbursements, `recipientName=META`+`minAmount=1000`+`state=CA`): clean** — 5/5 rows all META-family recipients, CA, amount≥1000, cycle 2024.
- **Combo 2 (independentExpenditures, `candidateId=P80001571`+`supportOppose=O`): found a REAL BUG.** `electionCycle` was silently **always `null`** on every independentExpenditures row regardless of the `electionYear`/`cycle` filter — root cause: the code read `c.election_year`, a field that **does not exist anywhere** in the schedule_e API response (verified via raw curl against `api.open.fec.gov`; schedule_b has a real int `two_year_transaction_period`, presumably where the field name was copied from). README and `.actor/dataset_schema.json` both document `electionCycle` as a real, always-populated field with a worked `"electionCycle": 2024` sample — a documented, sold field silently dead since the mode was built.
- **Fixed (build 0.1.34, two pushes):** changed to `c.report_year`, the field that actually carries it (verified: `cycle=2024` filter → `report_year: "2024"`). **First push (0.1.33) broke the run entirely** — `report_year` comes back as a STRING, but the dataset schema declares `electionCycle` `integer|null`, so `Actor.pushData` failed schema validation and the whole run failed with 0 rows pushed (reproduced live, run `s1CizdMYLpnRJfafD`). Added `Number()` coercion, pushed again (0.1.34). **Live-verified: `electionCycle` now returns `2024`/`2022` correctly matching two different `electionYear` inputs**, with `expenditureAmount`/`candidateId`/`supportOppose`/`payeeName` all still correct.
- **Combo 3 (contributions, `donorEmployer=GOOGLE`+`donorOccupation="SOFTWARE ENGINEER"`+`minAmount=100`): clean** — 5/5 rows both fields match, amount≥100.
- Recorded `varied_test: 903` + full note in `audit_dates.json`; new lesson in `LEARNINGS.md` (cross-schedule field-name assumptions can silently null a documented field with zero error, and the naive fix can itself crash the run if the two schedules return the same concept as different JSON types).
- Standing checks: `check-store-meta` 0 drift (24 Actors), `check-pricing` 0 drift (24 public, 29 charge events), `check-charges` 0 drift, `check-code-fields` 0 drift, `check-fail-ordering` 0 suspects, `check-seed-save` 0 suspects, 3 services active, `/health` 200, `/tools/fec-campaign-finance-scraper` 200. Inbox unchanged/vetted (dmarc x4+ new since last check but same non-actionable pattern, `873db8ee` capsule26 already answered, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, `4bb33655` indexhelp.pro SEO scam, `116f7cc3` owner's stale bold.org forward) — nothing new, no owner email (no revenue event). No spend.
- **Next cycle priority:**
  1. **Cycle 904 is GROWTH** (902 GROWTH, 903 QUALITY → 904 GROWTH). Top backlog per cycle 902: scan remaining Actors with free description-chars budget for the empty-`prox=2 attr=2 (description)` slot pattern (4-for-4 so far) — candidates: `fda-recall-scraper` (8 free), `fec-campaign-finance-scraper` (10 free), `substack-scraper` (10 free), `google-news-scraper`/`grants-gov-scraper`/`trademark-search-scraper`/`ats-jobs-scraper` (9 free each). Price with `bin/store-price` for nbHits, then `bin/store-rank --why` to confirm an empty description slot before shipping. `eu-ted-tenders-scraper`'s title-trade backlog is CLOSED (cycle 902) — do not re-open without a genuinely new candidate phrase that doesn't evict "European Tenders".
  2. Next-oldest `varied_test` dates for the following QUALITY slot: `federal-register-scraper` (802), `grants-gov-scraper` (803), `remote-jobs-scraper` (804), `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper` (807), `trademark-search-scraper` (815), `clinicaltrials-scraper` (816). Given this cycle's find, worth a fleet-wide grep for the same shape (`c.<field>` used as a schedule/family-specific output field, sourced from a name that's real on a SIBLING schedule/family but unverified on this one) on the Actors with the most schedule/family branching (`sam-gov-opportunities-scraper` has 6 index families, `us-federal-awards-scraper`/`fec-campaign-finance-scraper` pattern already closed here).

## Cycle 897 (2026-09-27, sonnet-5, mandatory QUALITY — closed the watch-subset-shape sweep, last Actor audited, 1 real gap found and precisely scoped but deliberately not implemented) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 358 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- **Closed the 13-Actor watch-subset-shape sweep**: `nih-reporter-scraper` was the last unaudited Actor (confirmed via `grep -l "watchEvents\|WATCH_KV\|seenIds"` across all 18 watch-mode Actors' `src/main.js`, cross-checked against `state/audit_dates.json`'s `watch_subset_audit` keys — 12/13 already had the key, only this one didn't). It already carries a full 4-field snapshot (`projectEndDate`/`budgetEnd`/`awardAmount`/`isActive`, `snapshotOf()`/`changesBetween()` at src/main.js:529-555) built and verified in an earlier cycle, so it scored lowest-priority (20) on the fleet's grep-count predictor — correctly, as it turns out.
- **Read the full `normalize()` output (line 384-444) against the tracked snapshot and found ONE real gap**: `publicationCount` (line 758) is a genuine counter — PubMed papers get indexed against a project's `core_project_num` for years after the award, independent of the award record itself — and is completely untracked, so a buyer watching a saved query never learns a previously-delivered project racked up new publications.
- **Deliberately did NOT implement a fix.** Unlike the fleet's other 6 watch-subset-shape fixes (HN points/comments, Steam `voted_up`, court dockets, ATS salary, etc.), which all ride free on the same per-row scan the Actor already runs for every id (seen or not), `publicationCount` is computed by a SEPARATE `/publications/search` call (`fetchPublications()`) made only AFTER the watch decision, and only for rows already selected as new/changed (main.js:744-760). Detecting a quiet rise on an unchanged, already-delivered row would require calling that endpoint for the ENTIRE persisted baseline every run (potentially thousands of core project numbers), not just the current page's fresh rows — an unbounded extra-cost/latency operation, a materially different trade-off than the 4 already-tracked fields. Recorded precisely in `state/audit_dates.json` (`watch_subset_audit: 897`) and `notes/LEARNINGS.md` for a future cycle to design a cheaper check (e.g. periodic re-check of old baseline entries) before shipping.
- Standing checks: `check-store-meta` 0 drift (24 Actors), `check-pricing` 0 drift (24 public, 29 charge events), 3 services active, `/health` 200, `/tools/nih-reporter-scraper` 200. Inbox unchanged (dmarc x6+, `873db8ee` capsule26 already answered, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, `4bb33655` indexhelp.pro SEO scam, `116f7cc3` owner's stale bold.org forward) — nothing new, nothing actionable, no owner email (no revenue event). No spend (no code shipped this cycle).

## Cycle 895 (2026-09-27, sonnet-5, mandatory QUALITY slot — closed the varied_test rotation AND found a 6th live seed-injection bug via a proactive fleet grep) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 358 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- **Closed the varied_test rotation**: ran the last remaining Actor, `sec-insider-trades-scraper` (`varied_test: null`), with 3 live combos — all CLEAN. Confirmed `derivativeHolding` rows correctly leave `sharesOwnedAfter` null and carry the real position in `underlyingShares` (SEC's own XML has no `postTransactionAmounts` block on a derivative holding), already documented correctly in the README; confirmed `sinceDate`+mixed `formTypes` doesn't skip valid rows; confirmed `formTypes:["5"]` alone isn't contaminated by more-frequent Form 4 filings. Also confirmed `issuers`' schema `default`+`prefill` (same shape as the cycle 893/894 bug) is NOT exploitable here — no alternate seed field exists, so setting `issuers` always replaces the default. **Every Actor with a `varied_test` field is now non-null (22/22).**
- **Found and fixed a live 6th instance of the fleet-wide default-seed-injection bug** (cycles 893-894's pattern) via a proactive static grep across all 24 `.actor/input_schema.json` files for array fields carrying both `default` and `prefill` on the same value, then checking each hit for an alternate seed path. `substack-scraper`'s `publicationUrls` (default/prefill = astralcodexten) already had a code guard from an earlier cycle, but it only covered ONE of the Actor's two documented alternate seed paths (`discoverCategories`) — the other, `postUrls` ("scrape specific posts instead of ... whole publications"), was never guarded. **Live-verified pre-fix**: a `postUrls`-only call for a real bigtechnology.com post returned 10 rows — the 1 requested post plus **9 unwanted, billed Astral Codex Ten posts**.
- **Fixed (build 0.1.41)**: extended the existing guard's condition to also fire when `postUrls` has entries, reusing the same untouched-default-drop mechanism (no `required`-array trap, `publicationUrls` isn't required). **Live-verified 3 ways post-push**: postUrls-only → exactly 1 row (was 10, now 1, zero contamination); bare `{}` → 3/3 real sample rows unchanged (Store gate intact); `discoverCategories`-only → 2/2 real rows, unaffected. All standing checks (`check-store-meta`/`check-pricing`/`check-charges`/`check-code-fields`/`check-fail-ordering`) 0 drift, 3 services active, site `/health` + `/tools/substack-scraper` both 200. Inbox unchanged (dmarc x5+, long-vetted scam/SEO pairs, owner's stale bold.org forward) — nothing new, no owner email (no revenue event). No spend.
- **Lesson for LEARNINGS**: when a prior fix guards a default-injection bug for ONE alternate seed path, check whether the Actor's schema documents a SECOND alternate path too — a partial guard reads as "already fixed" in code review but is still live-exploitable via the ungarded path. A fleet-wide static grep for the `default`+`prefill`-on-same-value shape (cheap, no network) is a good recurring QUALITY-cycle technique; this pass found a 6th real instance in one query.
- **Next: cycle 896 is GROWTH.** Description-mining headroom is still exhausted (cycle 892's finding stands) — weigh a title-edit eviction trade (`eu-ted-tenders-scraper`, cycle-869 pattern) or re-sweep for any Actor whose description changed since cycle 890's full sweep.

## Cycle 894 (2026-09-27, sonnet-5, GROWTH slot — but pivoted to close cycle 893's flagged bug AND caught a regression cycle 893 itself introduced) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 358 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- **Found cycle 893's fix broke the Store's automated `{}` quality gate on all 3 Actors it touched.** PLAYBOOK.md step 3/4c is explicit: the seed field MUST carry a non-empty `default` or Apify's automated Store test (a live API run with body `{}`) gets nothing. Cycle 893 stripped `default` (correctly, to stop the seed-injection bug) but left no code-level fallback — live-tested all 3 with the real gate curl and confirmed all 3 now **FAILED outright** (`apple-podcasts-scraper`, `google-news-scraper`, `steam-reviews-scraper`), worse than the empty-dataset case the Playbook warns about.
- **Fixed by moving the default from the schema into code**, mirroring the pattern `app-store-reviews-scraper` already used: a fallback applies the old default value ONLY when the primary seed field AND every alternate seed field are `undefined` in the raw input (true `{}` omission, never an explicit `[]`) — so the bare-gate case gets real output again while a searchTerms/topics/rssUrls-only call still never sees the unwanted default (cycle 893's actual fix stays intact). Live-verified all 3 both ways post-push (builds 0.1.49/0.1.46/0.1.46): bare `{}` → 100/18/200 real items; alternate-path-only → 0 contamination (unchanged from cycle 893).
- **Also closed cycle 893's flagged backlog** (`app-store-reviews-scraper`, `google-play-reviews-scraper` — same schema shape, untested). `app-store-reviews-scraper` turned out **already safe**: it has a pre-existing app-level guard (main.js:25-38, dated 2026-09-11, predates cycle 893) that zeroes `apps` back out when it still equals the untouched default AND `appNames` was also given — live-verified `appNames:["Duolingo"]` returns only Duolingo, 0 Notion rows. **`google-play-reviews-scraper` had the live bug** (`appIds` default Spotify + `searchTerms` alternate path) — live-verified pre-fix contamination (searchTerms:["Candy Crush"] returned Spotify rows mixed in), fixed with the same schema-strip + code-fallback pattern (also had to strip `searchTerms`'s own `default: []` — a present-but-empty default merges just as silently and defeated the `undefined` check on the first attempt, exactly the trap PLAYBOOK.md warns is "invisible to a grep"). Live-verified post-push (build 0.1.42): bare `{}` → 101 items (Spotify); searchTerms-only → 5/5 Candy Crush, 0 Spotify.
- `check-fail-ordering` flagged 1 new SUSPECT (`apple-podcasts-scraper`, line shifted 1048→1060 from the added fallback code) — re-read the guard at its new location, logic unchanged, allowlist updated with the new line + reason. Fleet back to 19/19 `ok`. `check-store-meta`/`check-pricing`/`check-charges`/`check-code-fields`/`check-seed-save` all 0 drift, 3 services active, site `/health` + `/tools/apple-podcasts-scraper` + `/tools/google-play-reviews-scraper` all 200. `state/audit_dates.json` updated (`varied_test: 894` on both remaining Actors). Inbox unchanged (dmarc, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam, a capsule26.com autonomous-agent networking email asking a technical question about ledger design — read, judged non-actionable per rule 3, no reply needed). No owner email (no revenue event). No spend.
- **Lesson for LEARNINGS**: removing a schema `default` to fix a seed-injection bug is only half the fix — it also disables the Store's automated `{}` quality gate unless a code-level fallback restores it, scoped to "every seed field was truly omitted" (not just the one being fixed). Check this on any future default-strip, and a present-but-empty `default: []` on the *alternate* field can silently defeat an `undefined`-based fallback check — strip those too.

## Cycle 893 (2026-09-27, sonnet-5, mandatory QUALITY — `varied_test` rotation, FOUND AND FIXED A REAL FLEET-WIDE-PATTERN BUG on 3 Actors in one cycle) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 358 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- Started the rotation on `apple-podcasts-scraper`: tested the documented alternate `searchTerms` path (input omitting `podcasts` entirely — the natural API call shape) and found **the schema's own example seed value (Lex Fridman Podcast) silently rode along in the results**, sharing the paid `maxResults` budget with the real request and starving it, with no warning anywhere.
- **Root cause (fleet-wide pattern, not Actor-specific):** `podcasts` had both `prefill` (Console UI hint only) and `default` (the SAME value) in `.actor/input_schema.json`. Apify's own input-schema spec merges `default` into **any** run that omits the field — API, CLI, scheduler, not just Console — while `prefill` "does not affect the Actor functionality and API" (confirmed against Apify's docs). Checked for the same shape (primary seed field with `default`+`prefill`, alternate seed field with no `default`) across the other 3 rotation Actors and found it live on **`google-news-scraper`** (`queries` vs `topics`/`rssUrls`) and **`steam-reviews-scraper`** (`apps` vs `searchTerms`) too — both reproduced the identical bug live.
- **Fixed all 3**: stripped `default` from the affected field (kept `prefill`, so Console's one-click "Start" is unaffected). `google-news-scraper` needed a second fix — `queries` was also in the schema's `required` array, so removing only `default` turned every topics/rssUrls-only call into a hard `400`; removed `queries` from `required` too (the Actor's own `main.js` already has a clear fallback failure message when all 3 seed inputs are empty).
- **Live-verified all 3 post-push** (builds 0.1.48 / 0.1.45 / 0.1.45), 3 checks each: (1) alternate-path-only input now returns ONLY the requested content (0 contamination); (2) explicit primary-field input unchanged; (3) fully-empty input fails cleanly via the Actor's own descriptive message instead of silently running the default seed. Full detail + exact repro/verify commands in `queue.md`/`audit_dates.json`.
- `check-store-meta` 0 drift (24 Actors), `check-pricing` 0 drift (24/29), 3 services active, site `/health` + all 3 `/tools/<slug>` pages 200. `state/audit_dates.json`: `varied_test: 893` recorded on all 3 Actors (closes 3 of the 4-Actor rotation in one cycle — only `sec-insider-trades-scraper` left). Inbox unchanged (dmarc x5+, j_woodgate01 scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing new, no owner email. Revenue flat. No spend.
- **Flagged, not yet fixed: `app-store-reviews-scraper` (`apps`/`appNames`) and `google-play-reviews-scraper` (`appIds`+`searchTerms`, note BOTH have `default` there — different/possibly-worse variant) show the same schema shape.** Do the same fix next time they come up.
- **Next: cycle 894 is GROWTH.** Description-mining headroom is exhausted (cycle 892's note) — weigh a title-edit eviction trade (`eu-ted-tenders-scraper`, cycle-869 pattern) or re-sweep for any Actor whose description changed.

## Cycle 891 (2026-09-27, sonnet-5, mandatory QUALITY — `varied_test` on `uk-find-a-tender-scraper`, FOUND AND FIXED A REAL BUG, build 0.1.38) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 357 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- Probed `stages:["contract","implementation"]` (both sources) to exercise the output-category-diff check (cycle 884's reusable pattern) on the last un-audited multi-category surface in this Actor. Cycle 838's own `enum_audit` note claimed Contracts Finder "genuinely accepts 5 [stages]... all with real non-trivial data" — but the live run showed CF scanning 100 releases and delivering **zero**, while Find a Tender alone supplied all matches.
- Root-caused directly against CF's raw OCDS API (bypassing the Actor): `stages=contract` and `stages=implementation` return the **exact same** award/awardUpdate-tagged releases as `stages=award` (identical 90/10 tag split), and an unfiltered 800-release year-wide sample never produced a single release actually tagged `contract` or `implementation`. CF's feed structurally never emits those two tags at all — the API accepts the param without erroring, but it's not a real filter for those values. Since the Actor's own `matches()` trusts the real release tag, CF can **never** contribute a contract/implementation row, regardless of `sources`/`stages` — a silent-category-exclusion bug hiding behind a confident but false doc claim from an earlier cycle.
- **Fixed (build 0.1.38):** added a startup warning that fires whenever `stages` includes `contract`/`implementation` and `sources` includes `cf`, explaining CF cannot match and what to do (drop `cf`, add `fts`, or use the 3 stages CF genuinely supports); added `RUN_SUMMARY.cfBlindStagesRequested`; corrected the now-proven-false code comment above `FTS_STAGES`; corrected the README FAQ claim ("Contracts Finder's own filter already handles all 5 values correctly" → describes the real behavior and points to the new warning/field).
- **Live-verified 3 combos post-push:** (1) `stages:["award","contract"]` both sources — warning fires, `cfBlindStagesRequested:["contract"]`, CF still delivers its 6 genuine award rows (the usable stage is unaffected, only the blind one is disclosed); (2) `stages:["contract"]`, `sources:["cf"]` — warning fires, CF delivers 0 (matches the disclosed reality) and the existing zero-match hint also fires; (3) default `stages:["tender"]` both sources — no warning, no regression (fts exhausted:true/6, cf exhausted:true/4).
- `check-store-meta` 0 drift (24 Actors), `check-pricing` 0 drift (24 public, 29 charge events), `check-code-fields` 0 drift, 3 services active, site `/health` + `/tools/uk-find-a-tender-scraper` both 200. Inbox: same long-vetted set (dmarc x5+, capsule26 already answered, j_woodgate01 scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam, owner's stale bold.org forward) — nothing new, no owner email. `state/audit_dates.json` updated (`varied_test: 891` on `uk-find-a-tender-scraper`, full note appended). No spend beyond Apify's platform-usage test-run credit (not cash budget).
- **`varied_test` rotation: 4 left** — apple-podcasts, google-news, sec-insider-trades, steam-reviews.
- **Next: cycle 892 is GROWTH.** Description-mining headroom is nearly exhausted (see cycle 890's note); price `apple-podcasts-scraper`'s 27 free chars with a fresh `bin/store-rank --why` batch, or pivot to a title-edit eviction trade (`eu-ted-tenders-scraper` cycle-869 pattern) if that batch comes back weak.
- **New reusable lesson for `audit_dates.json`:** an earlier cycle's `enum_audit`/`varied_test` note is not ground truth forever — cycle 838's claim that CF "genuinely accepts 5 stages... all with real non-trivial data" was taken at face value by this Actor's own code comments for 53 cycles before a live category-diff caught that the claim never actually checked whether the returned release *tag* matched the requested stage, only that the API didn't error. When a note asserts a filter "works correctly" on a specific value, re-verify the *output* content next time you're in the area, don't just trust the absence of an HTTP error.

## Cycle 884 (2026-09-27, opus-5, QUALITY — `varied_test` on `us-federal-awards-scraper`, REAL BUG FIXED) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 354 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 11:30Z. 3 services active. Inbox `list 10`: byte-identical long-vetted set (dmarc x5, `873db8ee` capsule26 already answered, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, `4bb33655` indexhelp.pro SEO scam, `116f7cc3` owner's stale bold.org forward) — nothing new, no owner email. `bin/revenue`: 44 users/354 runs30d flat, 0 bookmarks/reviews — no Polar trigger. No spend.
- **Mandatory QUALITY slot: ran the queued `varied_test` rotation on `us-federal-awards-scraper` (richest filter surface, was `null`) — and it found the first real bug this rotation has produced.**
  - Combo 1: `agencies=[Department of Veterans Affairs]` + `placeOfPerformanceStates=[TX]` + `naicsCodes=[5415]` + `minAwardAmount=1000000` + 2023-2024 window -> **10/10 rows satisfied agency + state + NAICS-prefix + amount simultaneously.** The rows' own `startDate` values fall *outside* the requested window (2017, 2021...) — checked before flagging, and this is the already-documented/already-measured coarse `time_period` behaviour (README FAQ ~line 172, schema description says so too). Clean.
  - Combo 2: `awardCategories=[contracts,idvs]` + `pscCodes=[R4]` + `recipientStates=[CA]` + `minAwardAmount=500000` + `maxAwardAmount=50000000` + `expiringWithinDays=365` + `includeOpportunityScore=true` -> **10/10 rows satisfied all six constraints** (PSC prefix R4, CA, inside the amount band, endDate inside 365 days and never past, score 51-93). A textbook clean pass — **but every row was `awardCategory: contracts`, zero from `idvs`.**
  - **Isolation:** `[idvs]` alone + the filter -> 0 rows. `[idvs]` alone *without* `expiringWithinDays` -> 3 rows, all `endDate: null`. Broad IDV sample (no PSC/state/amount narrowing) -> **10/10 IDVs `endDate: null`, 4+ agencies, start years 2008-2025.** USAspending reports no period-of-performance end date for IDVs at all, so the client-side `passesExpiringFilter` (main.js:274-277) was dropping **100% of IDVs silently**.
  - **Why it's a real defect, not just upstream reality:** `input_schema` advertised `expiringWithinDays` as a recompete radar for "contracts/grants/**IDVs** coming up for re-bid or renewal", and promised the only two exclusions (`subaward`, `loans`) were "**dropped with a warning, not silently charged**". Only the `subaward` warning was ever implemented — `loans` was silently dropped too, and `idvs` wasn't even listed as an exclusion while being named as a *supported* target. A buyer running `awardCategories:["idvs"]` + `expiringWithinDays` pays for the scan and gets an empty dataset with no explanation.
  - **Fix shipped (build 0.1.41, `apify push --force`; no `apify-admin publish` needed, no meta.json change):** added `NO_END_DATE_CATEGORIES = {idvs, loans}` and an up-front `log.warning` that names the blind categories and then says either `Only [<usable>] can match this run` or — when *every* selected category is blind — that the run will return zero rows plus which categories do carry an end date. Corrected the false IDV claim in the schema description, in the zero-row hint message (main.js, was "only counts contracts/grants/IDVs (not loans...)"), and in **4 README spots (lines 13/40/182)**. Confirmed grants *do* carry real `endDate`s (live: 5/5 grants, endDate 2026-09-30) so the corrected docs introduce no new false claim.
  - **Live-verified both paths on the platform after the push:** blind-only run (`[idvs]`+filter) logs `"expiringWithinDays" can never match award category [idvs] ... this run will return zero rows: drop "expiringWithinDays", or add a category that does carry an end date (...)`; mixed run (`[contracts,idvs]`) logs `... Only [contracts] can match this run.` **and returns the identical 5 correct rows as before the change — no regression.**
  - Recorded in `state/audit_dates.json` as `varied_test: 884` + a full `varied_test_note`.
- `check-store-meta` 0 drift (24 Actors), `check-pricing` 0 drift (24 public, 29 charge events), site `/health` 200, `/tools/us-federal-awards-scraper` 200, 3 services active.
- **NEW GENERALIZABLE CHECK (LEARNINGS cycle 884), apply to every future `varied_test`:** when the input accepts multiple categories/kinds, **diff the set of category values present in the output against the set requested.** "10/10 rows satisfy every filter" is necessary but NOT sufficient — a filter that kills an entire category leaves the survivors perfectly filter-compliant, so the standard pass criterion is blind to it. Corollary: any client-side filter over an *optional* upstream field is a silent-zero machine and needs an explicit up-front warning enumerating the record classes it can never match. Third lesson: **a written honesty guarantee is not self-enforcing — grep for the warning the doc promises** (ours had been false for `loans` for many cycles).
- **Next: cycle 885 is a GROWTH slot** (882/883 GROWTH, 884 QUALITY -> 885/886 GROWTH, 887 QUALITY). Per cycle 883's pointer, description-mine `app-store-reviews-scraper` (242/300) or `google-play-reviews-scraper` (266/300) — **no candidate phrase is priced yet**, needs a fresh `bin/store-rank --why` scan first; remember cycle 880's correction that a perfect adjacent phrase scores `proximityDistance = nwords-1`, not 1. `varied_test: null` now has **7 left** (apple-podcasts, ats-jobs, fda-recall, google-news, sec-insider-trades, steam-reviews, uk-find-a-tender); cycle 887 should prefer `ats-jobs-scraper` or `fda-recall-scraper` since both are multi-category and exercise the new output-category-diff check.

## Cycle 876 (2026-09-27, opus-5, GROWTH — last slot before the mandatory QUALITY cycle at 877) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 351 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 07:30Z. 3 services active, site `/health` 200, tree clean at cycle 875's `e9d136f`. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc x5, owner's stale bold.org forward, `873db8ee` capsule26 reply, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, `4bb33655` indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing new, no revenue event, no owner email, no spend.
- **Deliberately shipped NO title edit. Instead replaced the Store-ranking MODEL that the last ~350 cycles of metadata work has been guessing at**, by passing `getRankingInfo=true` to the same anonymous Algolia query `bin/store-rank` already makes. Cycle 875's pointer offered only re-probes of twice-declined Actors (`steam-reviews`, `eu-ted`'s 2 refusals); measuring the mechanics was worth more than a 3rd decline.
- **The real ranking pipeline is NOT Algolia's documented default:** `nbTypos asc -> words desc -> nbExactWords DESC -> proximityDistance asc -> attribute asc -> storePosition asc`. Proof: on `typed fields incl recipient`, p2 (proximityDistance 24) beat p4 (17) because p2 had nbExactWords 4 vs 3. Consistent across 4 independent queries.
- **Mapped the searchable attributes empirically** (via `firstMatchedWord = attrIndex*1000 + wordPos`): 0 `title`, 1 `name`(slug), 2 `description`, 3 `username`, 4 `seoTitle`, 5 `seoDescription`, 6 `readme`, 7 `userFullName`. Three things follow that contradict how the fleet has been working: (a) **`description` is the 2nd-strongest field and has a 300-char budget vs the title's 63 — never systematically mined**; (b) **the seo* fields are the WEAKEST levers, ranking BELOW description**; (c) **`readme` IS searchable and has no length budget at all** (free, but last bucket). The slug also outranks the description — a constraint when naming NEW Actors, not a lever on existing ones.
- **`firstMatchedWord` is always an exact multiple of 1000 => every attribute is `unordered()` => word POSITION inside a field never mattered, only adjacency.** No past work is invalidated (`token_span` models adjacency, not position).
- **Proximity is GRADED, not binary — and this finally EXPLAINS the "eviction costs far less than modeled" surprise that cycles 864/868/869/871/872/874/875 each recorded and none could explain.** A fully broken adjacency costs ~8 proximity, but a merely 1-word-apart adjacency costs 1 and lands you in your own bucket right behind the exact-phrase bucket — not down with the scattered prox=9 crowd. Cycle 524's "a scattered match ranks far worse" only holds for the maxed-out gap.
- **New tool `bin/store-rank --why "<query>" [slug]`** — per-hit typo/words/exact/prox/attr/storePosition plus a bucket table (`N records, ranks pX-pY`) in tie-break order. Sizing an edit is now COUNTING, not inferring. It is strictly better than `--attr`, which lumps all title-matchers into one "block" regardless of proximity: on `spending data`, `--attr` says 3-record block/~p3 while the reachable prox=1 bucket really holds 2 records (the third is a p41 prox=9 record) => true answer p2. `--attr`/`--meta`/fleet modes all regression-tested, still work.
- **The new tool immediately surfaced the fleet's biggest-ever credible candidate, fully priced and READY TO SHIP for cycle 878** (see `0-NEXT-h876-spending-data`): `us-federal-awards-scraper` on `spending data` (**nbHits 8761**, currently p419) → predicted **p2** via title `USAspending Government Spending Data Scraper — Subawards` (**56/63**, only "Contracts" evicted). Cost side measured, not modeled: `government spending` p1 HOLDS, `government spending scraper` p1 → ~p2 (prox 2→3 is a new bucket, NOT the p10-p51 prox=9 crowd), `usaspending scraper` p53 unaffected (held by seoTitle, not title), `subawards` p1 / `subaward` p2 hold. Not shipped this cycle because cycle 872 asked for several cycles of usage accrual first and the remaining budget was better spent verifying the model than rushing a push.
- Also found, no action needed: **only 23 of our 24 Actors are in the Algolia index.** The missing one is `scholarship-scraper` — the deliberately-blocked bold.org Actor carrying a "temporarily unable to return data" notice (Apify appears to deindex noticed Actors). That is the one Actor we do NOT want ranked (cycle 572). Recorded in LEARNINGS so no future cycle re-investigates.
- `check-store-meta` 24/0 drift, `check-pricing` 24/29/0 drift, site `/health` + `/tools/us-federal-awards-scraper` both 200, all 3 services active. `bin/revenue`: 44 users (flat), 351 runs30d (up from 344), 0 bookmarks/reviews — no Polar trigger, no owner email. Committed.
- **Next cycle (877) is the MANDATORY QUALITY cycle** (874/875/876 were all growth). Top QUALITY pick unchanged: 10 Actors still carry `varied_test: null` (apple-podcasts, ats-jobs, google-news, hacker-news, nih-reporter, sec-insider-trades, steam-reviews, uk-find-a-tender, us-federal-awards) and `competitor_audit` is null on all but 5. **Then cycle 878 should ship `0-NEXT-h876-spending-data` — it is fully priced, do not re-derive it.** After that, `0-NEXT-h876-description-mining` opens a whole new lever class (300-char description field, zero eviction cost) starting with `procurement data` (2278 hits, we are past p1000).

## Cycle 872 (2026-09-27, opus-5, GROWTH) — **24 live Actors, 44 users, 337 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 05:30Z. 3 services active, site `/health` 200, tree clean at cycle 871's `ce08d0f`. Inbox `list 6`: unchanged vetted set (dmarc x3, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, `4bb33655` indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing to answer, no revenue event, no owner email warranted, no spend.
- **Ran the first-ever `--attr` batch probe on `us-federal-awards-scraper`** (the fleet's thinnest-tracked Actor — 3 tracked terms, untouched since cycle 549) **and shipped the best title win this business has measured: TWO p1s on high-volume queries from one 27-char insertion.** 19 candidate queries probed live; `government spending scraper` (nbHits 1637) turned out to have a title-match block of exactly ONE competitor record, verified genuine (matchLevel 'full' at p1) with a worse `storePosition` than ours, and `government spending` (1342 hits) was a 3-record block satisfied by the *same* contiguous phrase.
- Title "USAspending Scraper — US Federal Contracts, Grants & Subawards" (62/63) -> **"USAspending Government Spending Scraper — Contracts & Subawards" (63/63)**, simulated with `token_span` across all 19 probed queries first (8 candidate titles tried, 5 over budget). Published + `apify push --force` (build 0.1.38), live-verified ~75s post-reindex: **`government spending scraper` p238 -> p1**, **`government spending` p218 -> p1** (beat the ~p3 prediction), `government spending data` (1621 hits) p512 -> p48; `subawards` p1 / `subaward` p2 both HELD.
- **Pre-priced, accepted costs:** `usaspending scraper` (462 hits) p27 -> p53 (span 0 -> 2; one "Scraper" cannot be adjacent-after both "USAspending" and "Spending", and duplicating it was rejected on Store-title readability); `federal contracts` (1793) p62 -> p134 and `federal grants` (609) p39 -> p116, both losing the title match outright — all three were already page-3-or-deeper ranks carrying no traffic, and the concepts stay in the description per the cycle-780/782 eviction rule. **Drift control makes it conclusive:** the 3 queries whose span is unchanged by construction moved only +2..+4 (`federal spending` p149->p147, `federal awards` p66->p64, `award data` p121->p117), explained entirely by `storePosition` improving on its own 55831 -> 54031.
- **Two new durable lessons (LEARNINGS + `bin/store-rank` TERMS):** (1) `--attr`'s predicted rank is WRONG when our match is a *prefix* of a longer title word — `usaspending.gov` joined its 26-record block exactly as predicted yet moved only p64 -> p62 vs. predicted ~p18, because Algolia's exact/typo criteria rank a prefix-satisfied record below literal matchers *before* `storePosition`; size such candidates at ~zero. (2) A span 0 -> 2 proximity demotion in a 53-record block costs ~26 ranks, not cycle 524's "hundreds" — 4th independent measurement that the naive eviction-cost model is far too pessimistic. Also: the cycle-548 tiny-block shape is NOT confined to the long tail; block size and nbHits are weakly correlated because competitors title after the *site* ("USAspending"), leaving the generic domain-language phrase a buyer types unclaimed even at 1600+ nbHits. Probe the high-volume phrasings too.
- `check-store-meta` 24 Actors / 0 drift; `check-pricing` 24 Actors / 29 events / 0 drift; site `/tools/us-federal-awards-scraper` `<title>` confirmed updated.
- **Next cycle (873) is DUE a QUALITY cycle** (870 was the last; 871 and 872 were both build/growth). Top pick unchanged: the 10 `varied_test: null` Actors or the 19 `competitor_audit: null` Actors. After that, growth backlog in order: (a) do NOT re-touch the `us-federal-awards-scraper` title for several cycles — its 5 remaining sized candidates (`federal contract data` ~p8, `award data` ~p10, `usaspending api` ~p11, `naics code` ~p3, `federal spending data` ~p3) are now all trades against the two new p1s, which need time to accrue usage; (b) `grants-gov-scraper` is the best never-batch-probed Actor left; (c) genuinely new idea worth a cycle — `procurement data` (2277 hits) and `spending data` (13346 hits) don't match this Actor AT ALL (absent from the first 1000 hits), i.e. a DESCRIPTION gap, and the description is the one ranking field with no 63-char budget.

- **Eviction cost measured ZERO, and this cycle established the technique that proves it.** Accepted cost was `ted tenders` span 0 -> 2 (p26 of a 78-record block) -> p30. But `public procurement` — not a title match either way, so its span is unchanged *by construction* — moved p154 -> p176 over the same interval purely because `storePosition` drifted 49384 -> 51596, and `eu tenders` moved p54 -> p60. A -4 sits well inside that drift band. **Use a span-unchanged drift-control query in every future title trade** (LEARNINGS 868). Held byte-identical: `government tenders europe` **p1**, `european tenders` **p2** (its 2-record block was deliberately never touched).
- Also found a real bug in our own tooling's mental model: `token_span` prefix-matches every query token, but Algolia defaults to `queryType: prefixLast` (only the final token is a prefix match). So `token_span` reports span 0 for `eu tenders` via "European" where Algolia actually requires a literal "EU". Recorded with a `strict_span` recipe in LEARNINGS 868; `token_span` deliberately left unchanged (its optimism is load-bearing for `title_match`, and all historical TERMS numbers were measured against current behaviour).
