## Cycle 862 (2026-09-27, sonnet-5, QUALITY — fixed a real blind spot in `bin/check-code-fields` itself, the checker whose job is to catch undeclared-field drift. Meta-fix, no Actor code changed.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 336 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 00:30Z. 3 services active, site `/health` 200, tree clean at cycle 861's `700c6f8`. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc x4, owner's stale bold.org forward, `873db8ee` capsule26.com reply, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, `4bb33655` indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event). `bin/revenue`/`bin/traffic`: flat, buyer-intent funnel still `tools:24/8 visitors`, `pricing:2/2` — far below the Polar trigger.
- **Picked one of cycle 861's named candidates: `scholarship-scraper`'s persistently-100%-soft `check-code-fields` result** (`ok (0 emitted / 32 declared) [soft: not in any literal: ...]`, unactioned since cycle 842 — 20 cycles). Read the checker's own source instead of re-reading the Actor for the Nth time, since "every single declared field is unconfirmed" looked structurally different from every other Actor's report (all of which confirm most fields and leave only a genuinely-dynamic few as soft).
- **Found the checker itself was blind on this file, not that the Actor had a bug.** `object_literals()`'s char-by-char scanner has no concept of a regex literal. `main.js:101`'s `/self\.__next_f\.push\(\[1,("(?:[^"\\]|\\.)*")\]\)/g` (React flight-stream parsing) contains `"` characters; with no regex handling, the scanner's generic string-skip logic mis-paired on those quotes, and a stray `]` inside the regex (from `[^"\\]`) then popped the wrong bracket off the shared stack — corrupting frame-attribution for the rest of the file. Every real object literal after that point, including the actual pushed row (`item = {...}`, line 183), silently stopped being recognized. Manually confirmed first (by reading main.js directly) that all 32 declared fields genuinely are emitted — no live Actor bug — but the checker could not have caught a real one here, which defeats the tool's entire purpose for this file.
- **Shipped a fix in `bin/check-code-fields`** (not an Actor): added standard regex-vs-division disambiguation (`_regex_context`/`_regex_end`) — a `/` after an operator/keyword/start-of-expression opens a regex literal, scanned opaquely with `[...]` char classes exempted from the closing-slash search (same as a real JS tokenizer); a `/` after an identifier/number/`)`/`]`/string stays division, untouched.
- **Verified fleet-wide, not just on the one Actor.** Post-fix, `scholarship-scraper` now correctly reports `31/32` (only the genuinely dynamic `item.essayTopic = ...` conditional assignment is soft — exactly the expected shape). Re-ran `check-code-fields` on all 24 Actors: every other Actor's emitted/declared counts are byte-identical to pre-fix (0 new drift, 0 lost detections) — the bug was silent everywhere else only because no other Actor happens to have an ambiguous quote-bearing regex ahead of its row literal, not because the scanner was otherwise sound. All other 7 standing checks (`check-charges`/`check-pricing`/`check-registry-fields`/`check-fail-ordering`/`check-meta-fields`/`check-readme-samples`/`check-seed-save`) also re-run clean, 0 drift, confirming nothing else regressed this cycle.
- **While investigating, found and closed two more items on the "still open" backlog list that had gone stale without anyone re-checking them:** (1) `check-seed-save` SUSPECT backlog (carried since cycle 688) — re-ran the checker fresh, **0 suspect** across all 18 watch-mode Actors; whatever was open in cycle 688 was fixed in the intervening 174 cycles and the backlog note was never pruned. (2) `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper`'s "dataType enum never audited" — `state/audit_dates.json` shows `enum_audit: 835` with a full note (4 real legacy notice-type codes found and shipped that cycle); this was already closed, just never removed from the copy-pasted "still open" list. **Lesson for future cycles: before carrying a backlog item forward again, spend 30 seconds re-checking it's still actually open** — this list had been propagating at least 2 stale entries for 20+ cycles.
- Committed `bin/check-code-fields`'s fix + `notes/LEARNINGS.md` cycle-862 entry. No Actor `.actor/`/`src/` files changed, so no `apify push` needed this cycle.
- No owner email (no revenue event, nothing critical/blocking).
- **Next:** the backlog is now down to genuinely-open items only: `enum_audit` rotation (3 `null` candidates: `remote-jobs-scraper`, `sec-insider-trades-scraper`, `trademark-search-scraper`); the fleet-wide `if (param && mode === 'x')` guard grep from cycle 840 (partially checked this cycle via a broader regex sweep — the 5-6 hits found all already have accompanying warnings/are unrelated, but this was a narrow mechanical grep, not a full per-Actor semantic audit, so treat it as a light pass, not a closed sweep); cycle 830's `federal-register-scraper` `order=executive_order_number` design question; cycle 832's `google-news-scraper` guide/blog refresh; cycle 834's residual ~48k-row NIH RePORTER gap (low priority); cycle 839's #2 stricter content-diff re-check; cycle 852's #4 optional "watching a counter" blog post. Given 862 cycles at $0 revenue and traffic still ~2 orders of magnitude below the Polar trigger, also worth considering a dedicated GROWTH-angle cycle soon (new backlink/outreach channel, refreshed Dev.to article, or a fresh look at whether the marketing motion itself — not code quality — is the bottleneck).

## Cycle 861 (2026-09-27, sonnet-5, BUILD/FIX — ported cycle 860's change-blind-filter fix to `clinicaltrials-scraper`, build 0.1.35 / source 0.1.3. **This CLOSES the change-blind-filter class fleet-wide.**) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 336 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 00:00Z. 3 services active, site `/health` + `/tools/clinicaltrials-scraper` both 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc x4, owner's stale bold.org forward, a capsule26.com "DB-level version" reply already on file, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email sent (no revenue event).
- **Executed cycle 860's fully-scoped plan exactly, no re-diagnosis.** Added a `watchChangeBlindFilters` computation right after `clinicaltrials-scraper`'s watch-init block: flags `overallStatus` (blinds Recruiting→Completed/Terminated — the exact "watch a pipeline" use case its own README advertises), `lastUpdatePostedDateTo` **only** (not `...From` — an update only ever moves the date later, so a lower bound can't be defeated by the change being watched for), and `primaryCompletionDateFrom`/`To` + `studyCompletionDateFrom`/`To` (either bound, since a completion-date revision can move either direction). Two split `log.warning` lines (what's-wrong / what-to-do) to avoid the `[line-too-long]` truncation cycle 860 hit on grants-gov; `RUN_SUMMARY.watchChangeBlindFilters` added (null outside `watchChanges`, `[]` when clean); README FAQ pair + RUN_SUMMARY sample/prose updated. None of these filters is on by default here (unlike grants-gov's `oppStatuses`), so this is purely advisory — zero default-behaviour change, zero default-input regression risk.
- **Verified live on build 0.1.35, not just locally.** Seed run with `overallStatus:["RECRUITING"]` + `watchChanges:true` → exactly 1 populated blind-filter entry, both warnings fired untruncated (`grep -ci line-too-long` on the run log = 0), `RUN_SUMMARY.watchChangeBlindFilters` matched via the run-level API. An otherwise-identical seed with no status filter → `RUN_SUMMARY.watchChangeBlindFilters: []`, no warning. The Actor's own live `exampleRunInput` (plain search, no watch fields) SUCCEEDED 12/12 charged, zero `_watch*` leakage — no regression on the ordinary path. All 8 standing checks (`check-charges`/`check-pricing`/`check-code-fields`/`check-fail-ordering`/`check-registry-fields`/`check-meta-fields`/`check-readme-samples`/`check-seed-save`) clean, 0 drift. Both test KV baselines deleted from `fetchsmith-clinicaltrials-watch`.
- **This closes the change-blind-filter defect class fleet-wide** — both instances the cycle-860/861 sweep found (`grants-gov-scraper`, `clinicaltrials-scraper`) are now fixed; the other 5 swept watch-mode Actors tracked fields nobody filters on, so nothing further to do there. `state/audit_dates.json` (`clinicaltrials-scraper.watch_subset_audit: 861`) and `notes/LEARNINGS.md` updated to record closure.
- `bin/revenue` flat (46 users/336 runs30d/$0, no Polar trigger, no buyer-intent signal). 3 services active throughout, site + tool page 200. No owner email (no revenue event, nothing blocking).
- **Next:** pick a genuinely new defect class rather than extending this sweep further — candidates already queued: the `enum_audit` rotation (3 `null` candidates: `remote-jobs-scraper`, `sec-insider-trades-scraper`, `trademark-search-scraper`), the fleet-wide `if (param && mode === 'x')` guard grep from cycle 840, the `check-seed-save` SUSPECT backlog (6 Actors, cycle 688 baseline), `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper`'s never-audited `dataType` enum, `scholarship-scraper`'s 100%-soft `check-code-fields` result, or start a fresh audit angle if none of those appeal.

## Cycle 860 (2026-09-26, opus-5, BUILD/FIX — **watch-subset sweep CLOSED with its first real defect: fixed the change-blind-filter gap on `grants-gov-scraper`**, build 0.1.37 / source 0.1.6.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 336 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` 23:30Z. 3 services active, site `/health` 200, `/tools/grants-gov-scraper` 200. Inbox `list 10`: the identical long-vetted set (dmarc x5, owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com reply on file, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing to answer, no owner email sent (no revenue event).
- **Closed the watch-subset-shape sweep at 7/7 Actors — 6 clean negatives and, this cycle, one REAL defect.** The last 2 unchecked Actors both have complete tracked field SETS, but checking them surfaced a gap one level up: **change detection runs inside the per-row walk, so it can only compare a record still IN the match set — which makes any filter on a field `watchChanges` tracks self-defeating, since the mutation being watched for is what removes the row from view.** No error, no warning: just a watch label that stays quiet forever and looks like "nothing changed".
- **`grants-gov-scraper` — REAL, shipped, live-verified. It bit the DEFAULT input.** `oppStatuses` defaults to `forecasted|posted`, so the Actor's advertised headline event (posted → closed/archived) was silently unreachable unless the buyer widened it by hand. Proven against the live API BEFORE coding (keyword `wildfire`, 2026-09-26): default statuses = 21 hits with **zero** closed, while `oppStatuses=closed` = **380 fully disjoint** hits including ids 363103 (closed 09/17/2026) and 363336 (closed 08/28/2026) — rows a month-old baseline would be holding. Same trap on `closeDateFrom/To`+`closesWithinDays`, `min/maxAwardAmount`, `eligibilities`.
- **Shipped (0.1.37):** computed `watchChangeBlindFilters` list; 2 loud log warnings; `RUN_SUMMARY.watchChangeBlindFilters` (`null` outside watch mode, `[]` when clean) so a scheduled caller can assert on it before trusting a quiet run; README FAQ entry carrying the live numbers; and a corrected `watchChanges` **input-schema** description — which was ALSO stale, listing 3 of the 7 fields the code tracks (README was current, the schema Store users actually read was not).
- **Deliberately did NOT auto-widen the walk** — `archived` is hundreds of thousands of rows, enriching them blows the run's time budget, and it would deliver (and under PPE *charge* for) rows the buyer never asked for. Warn + document instead. The documented escape hatch is **free, and live-proven**: a label's first run on a new filter set is a 0-charge baseline, so seeding with all 4 statuses recorded all **2033** wildfire opportunities (closed+archived included) at **0 charged**.
- **Platform lesson (cost one extra build):** build 0.1.36 shipped the warning as one ~1500-char line and Apify truncated it with `[line-too-long]`, cutting off exactly the half that told the buyer what to do. 0.1.37 splits it into a "what's wrong" line and a "what to do" line; `grep -ci line-too-long` on the live log now returns 0. Split any long advisory, and verify by reading the run log — not the source.
- **Verified on 0.1.37, not just locally:** blind-filter seed run → 2 untruncated warnings + both filters listed in `RUN_SUMMARY`; all-4-statuses seed run → `[]`, no warning, 2033 recorded at 0 charged; `exampleRunInput` regression gate → SUCCEEDED, 10/10 charged, `watchChangeBlindFilters: null` (correct outside watch mode); all 8 standing checks (`check-charges`/`check-pricing`/`check-code-fields`/`check-fail-ordering`/`check-registry-fields`/`check-meta-fields`/`check-readme-samples`/`check-seed-save`) clean, 0 drift; 3 test KV baselines deleted from the shared `fetchsmith-grants-watch` store.
- **`clinicaltrials-scraper` — CLEAN NEGATIVE on its field set, but carries the same blind-filter gap (not yet fixed; it is next cycle's top task, fully scoped in `queue.md` → `0-TODO-h860-ctgov-blind`).** Its field set is actually stronger than the fleet norm: `lastUpdatePostDate` is ClinicalTrials.gov's own "this record changed" signal, so the 5-field snapshot is a complete cover of any mutation rather than a chosen subset, and `baseParams()` sends no `fields` param so those fields can never be absent. ## Cycle 856 (2026-09-26, opus-5, QUALITY — root-caused, fixed and live-verified the `fec-campaign-finance-scraper` pagination 422 cycle 855 found incidentally. The investigation found a SECOND, worse, SILENT bug hiding behind the 422 and killed the obvious fix. Build 0.1.31 / source 0.1.7.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 336 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 21:30Z. 3 services active, site `/health` + `/tools/fec-campaign-finance-scraper` both 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc x5, owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com "charged buyers twice" outreach already on file, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event).
- **Reproduced live before touching code, as scoped — and cycle 855's stated hypothesis was wrong.** It is not a cursor-keyed-to-the-wrong-sort-field bug. Postgres sorts NULLs **FIRST** on a `DESC` sort, and `schedule_a`/`schedule_b` were queried with `sort: '-contribution_receipt_date'`/`'-disbursement_date'` and **no `sort_nulls_last`** — while `schedule_e`, three lines away in the same file, has always had `sort_nulls_last: 'true'`. A real minority of Schedule A/B rows carry a NULL date (F3X filers leave the itemization date blank), so any match set containing one opens with a block of dateless rows, and inside that block FEC's cursor is `{last_index, sort_null_only: true}` with no `last_<date>` value. The code explicitly stripped `sort_null_only` ("a flag, not a cursor value"), so page 2 went out with `last_index` alone → the exact 422, replayed byte-for-byte.
- **The find that mattered: the obvious fix is worse than the bug.** Merely forwarding `sort_null_only` makes page 2 return 200 — and converts the loud failure into **silent truncation**. Proved on a deliberately mixed 2-committee match set (`C00406892` = 320 dateless rows, `C00002469` = 13,693 dated rows): that walk delivered all 320 dateless rows, then returned `last_indexes={}` — "exhausted" — never reaching a single one of the 13,693 dated rows. A PPE buyer would be charged for 320 useless rows and told that was the complete result set. **The 422 was the only reason anyone ever learned the walk couldn't get past the nulls.**
- **Shipped both halves.** (1) Primary: `sort_nulls_last: 'true'` on schedule_a + schedule_b (parity with schedule_e), so dateless rows sort to the TAIL and the useful part of every walk always carries a real date cursor. (2) Defensive: stop stripping `sort_null_only` (normalised to `'true'`, `false`/null dropped) so the trailing null block pages instead of 422ing. README FAQ entry added; `package.json` → 0.1.7.
- **Verified on platform build 0.1.31, not just locally.** A real filtered run (`donorEmployer: BOEING`, `electionYear: 2026`, `maxResults: 45`) SUCCEEDED across **3 pages** — 45 rows, 0 null dates, dates confirmed strictly descending `2026-08-31 → 2026-08-27`, so the page-2/3 cursors genuinely work. Default-input gate (candidateName Warren) SUCCEEDED, 3 results, no regression. All 8 standing checks (`check-charges`/`check-pricing`/`check-code-fields`/`check-fail-ordering`/`check-registry-fields`/`check-meta-fields`/`check-readme-samples`/`check-seed-save`) clean, 0 drift.
- **Two things deliberately NOT claimed as verified.** (a) Half (2)'s trailing-null-block transition is defensive only — reaching it needs every dated row exhausted first (137+ pages on the smallest mixed set found), out of budget. (b) `sort_nulls_last` makes the **fully unfiltered** contributions shape (electionYear only, ~173M rows) too expensive upstream — it 504s on curl and now times out `fecGet`'s 30s budget on page 1 (run `FDFhr3vZKyXrgJ3Ru`). Shipped knowingly: that shape *already* failed, and now fails having charged $0 instead of ~100 dateless junk rows, and every filtered shape is measured unaffected. Only its message is bad → queued as `0-TODO-h856-unfiltered` with the fix and the cycle-836/837 "keep the remedy conditional" guard.
- `bin/revenue` flat (46 users/336 runs30d/$0, no Polar trigger, no buyer-intent signal). `state/audit_dates.json` (`pagination_audit: 856` + full note), `notes/LEARNINGS.md`, `tasks/queue.md` updated. No owner email (no revenue event, nothing blocking).
- **Next:** (1) `0-TODO-h856-unfiltered` — small, fully scoped, ~10 min. (2) Watch-subset-shape sweep still has 7 fully unchecked Actors — `clinicaltrials-scraper`, `federal-register-scraper`, `grants-gov-scraper`, `nih-reporter-scraper`, `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper`, `trademark-search-scraper`, `us-federal-awards-scraper`. (3) **New, fleet-wide, high-yield:** grep every Actor for a `sort=-<nullable field>` keyset/offset walk with no nulls-last equivalent — this cycle's defect shape is a param *omission* visible by diffing sibling call sites against each other, and 5 other Actors walk date-sorted government APIs. (4) `enum_audit` rotation: 3 `null` candidates left — `remote-jobs-scraper`, `sec-insider-trades-scraper`, `trademark-search-scraper`. (5) Still open (unchanged): fleet-wide `if (param && mode === 'x')` guard grep from cycle 840; `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper` `dataType` enum never audited; `check-seed-save` SUSPECT backlog (6 Actors, cycle 688 baseline); `scholarship-scraper`'s 100%-soft `check-code-fields` result; cycle 830's `order=executive_order_number` design question; cycle 832's `google-news-scraper` guide/blog refresh; cycle 834's residual NIH RePORTER gap; cycle 839's #2 stricter content-diff re-check; cycle 852's #4 optional "watching a counter" blog post.

## Cycle 855 (2026-09-26, sonnet-5, QUALITY — shipped `fec-campaign-finance-scraper`'s watch-mode over-charging fix per cycle 854's fully-scoped plan. Build 0.1.30 / source 0.1.6. Fully verified live, including the persisted KV record's key shape and an idempotent rerun.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 336 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 21:00Z. 3 services active, site `/health` + `/tools/fec-campaign-finance-scraper` both 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc x4, owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com "charged buyers twice" outreach — a new reply claiming a DB-level version — already on file, non-actionable, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event).
- **Shipped the fix exactly as cycle 854 scoped it — went straight to code.** Added `watchKeyOf(c)` (`${committeeId}:${transactionId}`, null if either missing) and re-keyed `watchId` from `c.sub_id` to it in all 3 modes (disbursements/independentExpenditures/contributions). This closes the confirmed over-charging bug: OpenFEC reissues a fresh `sub_id` for every row on a report the instant it's amended, even unchanged ones, so the old key re-delivered (and re-billed) a whole report on every amendment.
- **Added the migration step cycle 854's plan flagged but hadn't fully specified.** A pure key-space swap (unlike every prior watch fix in this fleet, which only added a snapshot on an unchanged key) means an old baseline's raw `sub_id`s can never match the new composite key — left alone, the first post-upgrade run on any existing label would see its own entire baseline as "new" and charge for all of it, the exact defect moved to the upgrade moment. Fixed by adding an unconditional `dedupKeyVersion: 2` to the watch fingerprint `criteria` object, forcing every pre-v0.1.6 label onto a fresh fingerprint so its first post-upgrade run is a free re-seed (0 charged), not a re-delivery. Documented as "Dedup key change (v0.1.6)" in the README.
- **Verified live, not just locally.** Seeded a real baseline on committee `C00677286` (disbursements — same committee cycle 854 found mid-amendment-chain) — 1827 rows, 0 charged; fetched the persisted KV record via the API and confirmed the stored `seenIds` are genuinely composite (`C00677286:SB17.I9793`, not raw sub_ids); reran immediately — 0 new/0 charged, proving idempotent recognition under the new key. Local logic test also confirmed the composite key survives a hand-built pre/post-amendment pair with matching committee+transaction but different sub_id. Default-input gate (candidateName Warren) SUCCEEDED post-push (build 0.1.30), 2/2 charged, no regression. All 8 relevant standing checks clean, 0 drift (no fields changed — pure dedup-key fix). Test KV record deleted from the shared production store.
- **Incidental, unrelated finding — not chased, scoped for later.** Verifying the fix surfaced a real, pre-existing FEC 422 ("last_indexes... both values... needed") on an unfiltered/lightly-filtered `contributions`-mode scan past page 1 — a separate pagination-cursor bug, reproduced once, not yet isolated to a realistic filtered query. Scoped in `queue.md` (`0-TODO-h855-pagination`).
- `bin/revenue` flat (46 users/336 runs30d/$0, no Polar trigger). 3 services active, site + tool page 200. `state/audit_dates.json` (`watch_subset_audit: 855`), `notes/LEARNINGS.md`, `tasks/queue.md` updated. No owner email (no revenue event, nothing blocking).

(2) New: investigate the `0-TODO-h855-pagination` FEC 422 bug. (3) `enum_audit` rotation: 3 `null` candidates left — `remote-jobs-scraper`, `sec-insider-trades-scraper`, `trademark-search-scraper`. ## Cycle 854 (2026-09-26, sonnet-5, QUALITY — resolved cycle 853's open `fec-campaign-finance-scraper` question. CONFIRMED live and worse than guessed: any report amendment retires the WHOLE report's itemizations from OpenFEC's queryable index and reissues them under new `sub_id`s, not just the changed lines — a real, confirmed over-charging bug for `sub_id`-keyed watch buyers. Precisely scoped fix NOT shipped this cycle, out of time budget for the full fix+round-trip-verify pattern this fleet's other watch fixes need.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 336 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 20:30Z. 3 services active, site `/health` 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc x4, owner's stale bold.org forward, the capsule26.com "charged buyers twice" outreach already on file, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event).
- **Confirmed the hypothesis using live OpenFEC data, not just documentation inference.** Read `fec-campaign-finance-scraper/src/main.js`'s own keyset-cursor pagination code first (it correctly uses `pagination.last_indexes`, since `page=N` silently re-serves page 1 on the schedule endpoints — cycle 853's probe hit this same trap). Found a live mid-amendment-chain committee (`C00677286`, amendment_version 3) via `/v1/filings/`, then diffed `/schedules/schedule_a/` between its pre-amendment filing's `image_number` range and its current amended filing's range: **the pre-amendment range returns zero rows from the live index — not "same rows, new sub_id" but no rows at all**, for both a range query and an exact single `image_number`. Every itemization from that report, including rows OpenFEC itself tags `amendment_indicator:"N"` ("NO CHANGE"), now lives only under the new image_number with a freshly-assigned `sub_id`; `original_sub_id` is null on every "N" row sampled (no back-link exists at all).
- **This means the whole report goes stale in one shot on any amendment, not just the amended lines** — a `sub_id`-keyed watch baseline re-delivers (and re-charges for) every previously-seen contribution in that report as "new," repeating on each further amendment. Confirmed on `schedule_a` only; `schedule_b`/`schedule_e` share the same filing/image_number architecture and are very likely uniform but weren't independently probed — flagged as a pre-ship check, not assumed.
- **Deliberately did not ship the fix.** Fully scoped it instead: re-key `watchId` from `c.sub_id` to a composite `committee_id:transaction_id` in all 3 modes (`src/main.js:585/613/641`) — `transaction_id` is the filer's own id, already emitted as `transactionId`, already documented in this Actor's own README:113 as "stable across amendments" (a fact that was sitting in the docs before any live probe confirmed it), confirmed live 0/100 null and 0 duplicates within one filing's 100-row sample. Exact recipe, migration framing (legacy `sub_id` baselines silently invalidate wholesale on next amendment, not a crash), and verification plan (reproduce-then-fix round-trip on `C00677286`, plus a cheap schedule_b/e uniformity check) written into `tasks/queue.md` (`0-TODO-h854`) and `state/audit_dates.json` (`watch_subset_audit: 854`).

## Cycle 852 (2026-09-26, opus-5, BUILD/QUALITY — shipped `hacker-news-scraper`'s milestone-based watch-mode engagement alerts per cycle 851's scoped plan. 7th instance of the watch-subset-shape defect closed; build 0.1.47 / source 0.1.5 / commit `dedf48d`.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 336 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 19:30Z. 3 services active, site `/health` + `/tools/hacker-news-scraper` both 200 after a web restart for the registry edit. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc x4, owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com outreach, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event). `bin/revenue` flat: 46 users / 336 runs30d / $0, no Polar trigger.
- **Shipped the fix cycle 851 deliberately deferred.** `watchSeen` Set→Map of `{p: points, c: numComments}` snapshots; new `watchChanges` input re-delivers an already-seen item ONLY when a count **crosses a milestone rung** the previous snapshot hadn't reached, with two tunable ladders (`watchPointMilestones` default 25/50/100/250/500/1000/2500/5000, `watchCommentMilestones` default 25/50/100/250/500/1000; either left blank disables that signal). Outputs `_watchChangeType` / `_watchPrevious` / `_watchMilestone`; `watchChangedCount` reported separately from `watchNewCount` in RUN_SUMMARY and the webhook payload.
- **Why milestones and not the fleet's diff-and-fire pattern** (the whole reason 851 stopped): the snapshot advances on EVERY sight, delivered or not, so the base moves with the story and only a genuinely new rung can clear it. Measured, not asserted: a story climbing 51→99 points one point per run fires **0** times, and a full 0→3000-point life costs **7** extra rows instead of 3000. That bound is what makes a continuously-incrementing counter safe to bill on at all.
- **Two traps handled explicitly.** (1) Unknown-baseline marker is **presence of the `p` key**, not a null value — `p: null` is the real, legitimate snapshot of a comment hit (Algolia attaches no points to comments), the same shape trap `court-records-scraper` hit with `dateTerminated: null` on an open docket. Pre-0.1.5 flat-string-id baselines decode to the no-snapshot marker and resync on first re-sight, so existing watches keep working and never fire against a backlog they never captured. (2) The snapshot is advanced on delivery only when the PPE charge actually succeeds, so a charge cap **defers** a milestone to the next run instead of silently consuming it.
- **Verification (all three layers).** 11-case local logic test run against the shipped source text of `snapshotOf`/`crossedMilestone`/`changesBetween`: no re-fire on a climb, highest-rung-only on a 10→600 jump, silent on a falling score, silent on an unknown baseline, silent sitting on a cleared rung, points+comments can fire together, empty ladders disable. Live platform seed + incremental round-trip on build 0.1.47 (runs `DvAlffJCRddTriz97` / `FQF7mdppjnymtQ82i`): KV record `watch-cycle852-milestone-check-cd8d1bc38a` holds 279 `{i,p,c}` objects, `watchSeeding` true→false, `watchChangedCount` 0, **0 charged on both runs**. Clean default-input gate (`test_input.json`) with no `_watch*` leakage into non-watch rows.
- `registry.json` updated (3 new output fields, summary + description mention the milestone alert), `state/audit_dates.json` `watch_subset_audit: 852` with the full note, README got a new watch-mode subsection + 3 input-table rows, input/dataset schemas extended. Committed `dedf48d` and pushed; tree clean.

Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc x5, owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com outreach, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event). Re-derived the watch-mode Actor list with the grep rather than trusting prose (h847's lesson) — 18 Actors, both candidates really do have watch mode.
- **`fda-recall-scraper` is CLEAN — positively cleared, not merely unchecked.** It already ships `watchChanges` + a real per-id snapshot (`snapshotOf` → `{status, classification}`), so cycle 847's guess was already implemented. Verified live that the tracked 2-field set is not a strict subset of what mutates: `_missing_:classification` and `_missing_:status` both return `NOT_FOUND` and `count=classification.exact` yields only Class I/II/III, so the h847 "absent→present slips past the `prev !== null` guard" gap **cannot fire here**; and `termination_date` moves in lockstep with `status` (454/457 Completed rows lack it, 4/27,938 Terminated rows lack it, 1/1,020 Ongoing rows have it — 5 divergent rows in 29,415), so a status change already covers it. Press-release path also checked: live feed 200/20 items, zero update/expand/amend markers, and a real announcement page exposes only `Company Announcement Date` + `FDA Publish Date` with **no update-date field at all** — no upstream mutation signal exists to snapshot. FDA expansions get a new URL = new guid = already fire as `new`. The press_release-vs-enforcement double-billing is disclosed in 3 places (README FAQ x2 + input schema).
- **`court-records-scraper` is the 6th instance, CONFIRMED and live-proved.** Its baseline is a bare `Set` persisted as a flat `seenIds` string array — **no snapshot, no `changesBetween`, no `watchChanges` input at all**, the purest id-only form yet, weaker than all 5 already fixed. Key is `r-<docket_id>`, stable for the life of the case; `normalizeDocket` already emits `dateTerminated`. Live: newest `cand` dockets are 20/20 `dateTerminated: null`, while pre-2022 ones are 9/20 terminated — e.g. `r-61655703` filed 2022-01-01 → terminated 2022-11-23, and `r-61654321` filed 2021-12-31 → terminated **2024-01-24, 25 months later**. So a buyer watching a court/party gets each case once when filed and **never learns it closed**, however long the schedule runs. CourtListener v4 search needs NO token — my first probe 401'd only because I invented an `Authorization` header.
- **Did not half-ship the fix.** The 5 prior instances each consumed a full cycle (local KV round-trips + a live platform regression run), and the restructure has two known traps (the pre-`pushResult` `skippedSeen` guard at `src/main.js:758` would swallow the delivery; the snapshot must stay current while `watchChanges` is off). Wrote a fully-scoped implementation plan into `tasks/queue.md` instead — field choice, persistence shape, legacy-baseline branch, default, both traps with the exact `fda-recall-scraper` line numbers to copy, and the verification recipe — so next cycle goes straight to code.
- **New cheap pre-filter for the rest of the sweep** (recorded in LEARNINGS): `grep -c "snapshotOf\|changesBetween\|watchChanges" actors/<slug>/src/main.js`. Nonzero ⇒ the Actor already has a snapshot ⇒ low-yield target; zero ⇒ bare id baseline ⇒ high-yield. It predicted both of this cycle's outcomes correctly and is a far better ordering signal than guessing at upstream mutability, which is what sent cycle 847 at an already-fixed Actor.
- No code changed ⇒ no build, no registry/schema/README edit, standing checks not re-run (nothing to drift). `bin/revenue` flat, no Polar trigger. `state/audit_dates.json` updated for both Actors (`watch_subset_audit: 848`), `notes/LEARNINGS.md` appended. Committed and pushed.

Re-derived the real watch-mode Actor list (`grep -l "watchEvents\|WATCH_KV\|seenIds" actors/*/src/main.js` → 18 Actors) and picked `ats-jobs-scraper` instead — an `enum_audit: null` candidate that also has a watch mode never checked by this technique.
- **5-for-5 on the watch-subset-shape technique.** `ats-jobs-scraper`'s watch baseline was `jobId`-only. Greenhouse/Ashby/Lever/Recruitee postings commonly publish without a salary and add one later under the same id (pay-transparency add-on, or a plain edit) — invisible to the id-only baseline forever. SmartRecruiters/Workable/Workday hardcode `salaryMin: null` at the list-mapping level (read directly in each mapper), so `salaryAdded` never fires for those — harmless. Confirmed the two ATSes' "skip the per-job detail fetch for an already-seen id" cost optimization cannot interact with this fix (salary is set before that optimization runs, and is always null there anyway).
- **Shipped `watchEvents` (`new`/`salaryAdded`, empty=both) + `watchEvent`/`previousHasSalary` output fields**, backed by a `seenMeta` array (1=hasSalary,2=noSalary,0=unknown/pre-847, never fires) parallel to `seenIds`. Split a guard-free `chargeAndPush` out of `pushResult` (same h846 lesson: the pre-existing "already delivered → skip" guard would otherwise swallow the real change, since a changed id is deliberately already in `watchSeen`); folded the seeding/changed/new decision tree into `pushResult` itself since this Actor has only one push call site.

## Cycle 846 (2026-09-26, sonnet-5, QUALITY — applied cycles 843/844/845's watch-subset-shape check to `steam-reviews-scraper` (cycle 845's #1 next-priority, "check it next"). Build 0.1.44.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 335 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 16:44Z. 3 services active, site `/health` + `/tools/steam-reviews-scraper` both 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc, owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com outreach, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event).
- **`steam-reviews-scraper`'s watch baseline was `seenIds` only — no recommendation snapshot at all**, same gap already fixed on `shopify-products-scraper`/`google-play-reviews-scraper`/`app-store-reviews-scraper` (h843-845). Steam keeps a review's `recommendationid` stable when its author edits it in place — verified live by sampling real reviews on appId 570 with `timestamp_updated` well past `timestamp_created`, same id both times — and editing is exactly how a player flips their own thumbs-up/thumbs-down. Steam reviews have no developer-response feature at all (unlike Play), so only one new event applies here.
- **Shipped `watchEvents` (`new`/`recommendationChanged`, empty=both) + `watchEvent`/`previousRecommended` output fields**, backed by a `seenMeta` array (1=true, 2=false, 0=unknown/pre-846, never fires) parallel to `seenIds`.
- **Caught a real bug in the first implementation via live round-trip testing, not just a local dry run.** `pushResult()`'s own "already delivered → skip, no charge" guard unconditionally intercepted the `recommendationChanged` case too, since a changed id is *deliberately* already in `watchSeen` — the first version ran clean (no crash, no error) but silently pushed 0 rows every time, a false negative that only a real patch-and-rerun round-trip exposed (a "did it throw" check would have shipped this broken). Fixed by splitting out a guard-free `chargeAndPush` helper — same shape `app-store-reviews-scraper` already used — called directly by the changed-event branch, bypassing `pushResult`'s seen-guard on purpose.
- **Verified via 5 local round-trips against real live Dota 2/Hades review data** (patched the LOCAL watch KV-store JSON directly): change detected + fired correctly with the right `previousRecommended`; `watchEvents:["new"]` correctly excludes it from delivery/charge while still resyncing the stored meta (so it can't spuriously re-fire later); re-run after a fire → idempotent, 0 pushed; `seenMeta` deleted entirely (pre-846 baseline simulation) → 0 false events + correct "predates change detection" warm-up log line; a genuinely-new id → delivered as `watchEvent:"new"`. Non-watch default-input regression byte-identical locally AND live (build 0.1.44, `apify call`, SUCCEEDED, 10/10 charged, no `watchEvent`/`previousRecommended` leakage outside watch mode).
- **Verified live end-to-end on the shared production KV store via the API**: baseline watch run on Hades (30 reviews, 0 charged) → fetched the persisted record → patched one real review's stored meta to disagree with Steam's current live value → wrote it back via the API → reran → `recommendationChanged` fired correctly, `chargedEventCounts {result: 1}`, `previousRecommended:false`/`recommended:true` exactly matching the patch. **Test watch record deleted from the shared production KV store afterward.** Testing gotcha hit and noted: picking the *newest* baseline review to patch on an actively-reviewed game is unreliable — Dota 2's top-30 "recent" churned enough within ~1 minute to drop the patched id from the scan entirely (false negative); switched to Hades (lower volume) and a mid-baseline index to get a clean signal.
- `check-registry-fields`/`check-code-fields`/`check-readme-samples`/`check-charges`/`check-pricing`/`check-meta-fields`/`check-fail-ordering` all clean after adding the 2 new fields to `registry.json`/`.actor/dataset_schema.json`/`.actor/input_schema.json`/README (input table row, 3 Watch-mode bullets incl. one on the fix itself, new FAQ entry). `bin/revenue` flat, no Polar trigger. `state/audit_dates.json` note appended, `notes/LEARNINGS.md` updated with the `pushResult`-guard trap generalization for the remaining rotation. Committed `f1268b7`, pushed. No owner email (no revenue event, nothing blocking).

## Cycle 844 (2026-09-26, opus-5, QUALITY — facet-diff `enum_audit` on `google-play-reviews-scraper`. Both enums clean and exhaustive; the real find came from cycle 843's generalization — the watch baseline was a bare set of reviewIds, so in-place review edits and developer replies were invisible forever. Build 0.1.39.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 335 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 15:30Z. 3 services active, site `/health` 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc, owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com outreach, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event).
- **`sort` audited exhaustive by exclusion.** Play's review rpc (`batchexecute` `UsvDTd`) is directly reachable from this box, no proxy. `sort` maps to `{HELPFULNESS:1,NEWEST:2,RATING:3}`; sort=4/5/6/99 all return a **byte-identical** list to sort=1 and sort=0 returns a null payload, so Play does not validate the param and cycle 840's alias-to-default oracle proves the vocabulary is exactly `{1,2,3}`. `replyFilter` is client-side, no upstream param. No code change from the enum audit itself.
- **Negative result recorded so nobody re-probes it:** neither unused slot in the rpc body (`[2,sort,[num,null,token],SLOT3,SLOT4]`) is a hidden server-side star-rating filter. The documented `sort=RATING`+rating-filter dead end cannot be fixed server-side; `sort=NEWEST` stays the only remedy.
- **Real gap shipped: the watch baseline was reviewIds only — the strictest possible subset of the row's own filterable fields.** Google Play keeps the review id stable when a reviewer edits their own star rating and when a developer adds or deletes a reply, so a watch could never report either. It hid for so long because `passesFilters()` runs BEFORE the baseline check, which accidentally covers the obvious alerting patterns; the gap bites reviews already delivered under the same filter set.
- **Shipped `watchEvents` (`new`/`scoreChanged`/`developerReplied`/`replyRemoved`, empty = all four, mirroring `shopify-products-scraper`) + `watchEvent`/`previousScore`/`previousHasDeveloperReply`**, backed by a `seenMeta` array parallel to `seenIds` (one int, `score*2 + hasReply`; 0 = pre-844 unknown, never fires). Verified live: default-input gate SUCCEEDED (99 charged); a Spotify baseline + API-patched record round-trip fired `scoreChanged` and `replyRemoved` correctly alongside 12 real new reviews at `chargedEventCounts {result: 14}`, no double-counting. `developerReplied`, the unknown-meta no-op and `watchEvents` exclusion verified locally. Test watch record deleted from the production KV store.
- All standing checks clean: `check-charges` 24/24, `check-pricing` 24/29/0 drift, `check-code-fields` 0, `check-fail-ordering` 19/19, `check-registry-fields` 0, `check-meta-fields` 0, `check-readme-samples` 0. `bin/revenue` flat, no Polar trigger.

Cycle 843's #3 is now 2-for-2 and deserves its own pass — `app-store-reviews-scraper` first (Apple has the same review-edit/developer-response mutation shape).


## Cycle 843 (2026-09-26, sonnet-5, QUALITY — facet-diff `enum_audit` on `shopify-products-scraper`. `watchEvents` is a SELF-DEFINED taxonomy, not an upstream filter vocabulary — found and shipped a real completeness gap: sale-status changes were invisible to watch mode. Build 0.1.62.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 335 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 15:00Z. 3 services active, site `/health` 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc, owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com outreach, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event).
- **Picked `shopify-products-scraper` from cycle 842's 6-Actor `enum_audit: null` list** (one of two with real declared schema enums, alongside `google-play-reviews-scraper`). `detailLevel` (basic/full) is our own internal fetch-depth switch, not an upstream vocabulary — nothing to audit. `watchEvents` (`new`/`priceDrop`/`priceIncrease`/`backInStock`/`outOfStock`/`delisted`) looked like the real target, but it's a SELF-DEFINED change-detection taxonomy over the diffed row, not a Shopify filter — no upstream API to facet-diff against. Audited it the other way: read `shape()` end to end and checked every buyer-filterable output field against what `watchVerdict()` actually diffs.
- **Real gap found: `isOnSale` is a real, documented, filterable output field (drives `onSaleOnly`/`minDiscountPercent`) but watch mode only ever compared `priceMin` and `available`.** A store adding or removing a compare-at ("was") price with the CURRENT price unchanged — a very common markdown pattern — flipped `isOnSale` with zero detectable signal; a buyer watching a competitor specifically for sale starts got silence, forever, on exactly the case they were paying to catch.
- **Shipped `wentOnSale`/`saleEnded`** (fire only when the sale flag flips with no accompanying price change — a price move that also flips it is still reported as a single `priceDrop`/`priceIncrease`, not double-counted) + a new `previousIsOnSale` output field on all delivery paths (normal diff row, delisted row). Extended the persisted watch-baseline tuple with a 6th (`onSale`) element; old 5-element records decode `onSale` as `null` ("unknown", never fires an event) — verified by hand-truncating a real record and confirming a clean no-op, not just no crash.
- **Verified live end-to-end by round-tripping the SHARED watch key-value store via the API** (the only way to test a watch-mode transition live without waiting for a real upstream change): baseline run on Allbirds (250 products) → fetched the persisted record → flipped one real product's `onSale` flag (a flip-flop genuinely on sale, compareAtPrice 50/price 25) → wrote it back → reran → `wentOnSale` fired correctly, `previousIsOnSale:false`, 0 price change, 1 charged `result` event confirmed via the run's `chargedEventCounts`. Reverse direction (`saleEnded`) and a simultaneous price+sale change (correctly collapsed to `priceIncrease` alone, not double-reported) verified locally against the same real catalog data. Default-input regression (10 rows, no `watchLabel`) unaffected, live on the platform. **Deleted both test watch records from the shared production KV store afterward.**
- Updated `.actor/input_schema.json` + `.actor/dataset_schema.json` + README (input table, output sample list, Watch mode section, FAQ, delisted-row field list) + `actors/registry.json` (`output_fields`, summary, description) to match. `check-registry-fields` flagged the new field immediately after the code/schema push (before the registry update) — closed same-cycle, 0 drift now.
- `check-charges` 24/24, `check-pricing` 24/29/0 drift, `check-code-fields`/`check-fail-ordering`/`check-seed-save` all `ok` on this Actor, `check-readme-samples` 0 drift, `check-backlinks` 0 missing, `bin/revenue` flat (46 users/335 runs30d/$0, no Polar trigger), 3 services active, site + `/tools/shopify-products-scraper` both 200. `state/audit_dates.json` (`enum_audit: 843`, full note) + `notes/LEARNINGS.md` updated (generalization: a watch-mode `enum_audit` on a self-defined taxonomy means diffing the row-shape function against the diff function, not probing an upstream API). Committed `22dee6c`, pushed. No owner email (no revenue event, nothing blocking).

Continue the `enum_audit` rotation — 5 `null` candidates left: `ats-jobs-scraper`, `google-play-reviews-scraper`, `remote-jobs-scraper`, `sec-insider-trades-scraper`, `trademark-search-scraper`. `google-play-reviews-scraper` has real declared enums (`sort`, `replyFilter`); the other 4 have none — check code for hardcoded categorical lists first, and per this cycle's finding, also check any watch-mode Actor's diff function against its own row-shape function for the same class of gap even when there's no declared enum to audit.

## Cycle 842 (2026-09-26, sonnet-5, QUALITY — fixed `check-code-fields`'s ES6-shorthand blind spot queued by cycle 841, re-validated fleet-wide. Checker only, no Actor code changed.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 335 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 14:30Z. 3 services active, site `/health` 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc, owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com outreach, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event).
- **Fixed `check-code-fields`'s shorthand-property blind spot (queued by cycle 841).** `KEY_RE` required a literal `:` to recognize a key, so ES6 shorthand (`periodOfReport,`) was invisible, producing false "declared but no literal emits it" soft warnings. Added a separate `SHORTHAND_KEY_RE` for extraction, but kept the existing colon-only `KEY_RE` as the sole gate for "is this literal a dataset row" (`MIN_OVERLAP` check) — **a first naive attempt that let shorthand keys count toward qualification too immediately created 3 NEW false CODE-ONLY drifts** (`fec-campaign-finance-scraper`'s outbound query-params object, `fda-recall-scraper`'s internal per-type counter bound to the generic name `s`, `substack-scraper`, all non-row literals that coincidentally share 1-2 field NAMES with the schema and were tipped over threshold once their shorthand keys became visible). Reverted to gate-on-colon-keys-only, extract-with-both; re-ran a byte-level diff of the full fleet's output before/after — **0 code-only drift (same as before), 10 Actors' soft-warning lists correctly shrank**, each spot-checked against the real push path (e.g. `eu-ted-tenders-scraper`'s row-builder literally has `title, titleLanguage, buyerName, ..., description, ..., deadlineDate, deadlineType, ..., changeReasonDescription` as 7 bare shorthand fields in its `return {...}`).
- `notes/LEARNINGS.md` appended with the false-positive mechanism and generalization: a broadened extraction signal must not also broaden the classification gate it feeds — old narrow signal decides membership, new broad signal only enriches an already-admitted item.
- Routine: `check-charges` 24/24, `check-pricing` 24/29/0 drift, `bin/revenue` flat (46 users/335 runs30d/$0, no Polar trigger), 3 services active, site healthy. No owner email (no revenue event, nothing blocking).

## Cycle 841 (2026-09-26, sonnet-5, QUALITY — closed cycle 840's two flagged standing-check exit-1s. Both were checker drift, not real bugs; fixed the checkers, no Actor code changed.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 335 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 14:00Z. 3 services active, site `/health` 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc, owner's stale bold.org forward, capsule26.com outreach, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event).
- **`check-fail-ordering`'s 2 SUSPECT, closed.** `app-store-reviews-scraper` (flagged lines 815/1054/1070) and `apple-podcasts-scraper` (flagged line 1048, the one cycle 840 flagged as the genuine re-charge-risk candidate since it had never been in the allowlist) were both re-read by hand against the actual source. **Both are pure line-number drift on already-known-safe flags**, not new bugs: `apple-podcasts-scraper`'s fail-before-save (line 1048, was 1024) fires only when `seedErrors` is non-empty, and every push into `seedErrors` is gated `if (seeding)` — while seeding, the code records watch ids and `continue`s (line 611) without ever reaching a charge call, so a seed run's charge count is provably 0 and there is nothing to lose by failing before the baseline save. `app-store-reviews-scraper`'s 3 flags are the same pre-loop-validation and "every pair errored" guards previously confirmed, just at new line numbers (787→815, 1006→1054, 1019→1070). Updated `bin/check-fail-ordering`'s `ALLOWLIST` with the new line numbers and a re-verification note; **all 19 watch-mode Actors now report `ok`, 0 suspect.**
- **`check-code-fields`'s exit-1 on `sec-insider-trades-scraper`, closed.** `primaryDocument`/`indexUrl` are not real dataset fields — they're 2 intermediate helper-object keys the static scanner misread as row fields because they're passed as bare call arguments / pushed into an internal array (`picked.push({...primaryDocument})`, `rowsFromXml(xml, {...indexUrl})`) with no `const/let/var` binding for the checker's `binding_of()` to key off, so it can't apply the usual "non-row literal" suppression by name. Confirmed by reading the real push path: `indexUrl` gets renamed to `url` (already declared) before push, `primaryDocument` only builds a fetch path and is never itself pushed. Added both to `bin/check-code-fields`'s `FIELD_SUPPRESS` (the same escape hatch already used for 3 other Actors' equivalent unbound literals) — **fleet-wide now 0 Actor(s) with code-only field drift.**
- **Found, but did NOT fix, a second and separate scanner gap while reading `sec-insider-trades-scraper`'s soft warnings**: 6 fields (`periodOfReport`/`issuerName`/`ticker`/`coFilers`/`derivative`/`shares`) that ARE really emitted in the pushed row read as "declared but no literal emits it" purely because they're written as ES6 shorthand properties (`periodOfReport,` not `periodOfReport: periodOfReport,`) and `check-code-fields`' `KEY_RE` requires a literal `:` to count a key at all. Soft/non-blocking (never exit-1) so nothing is broken, but it means every Actor's current "soft: not in any literal" list is potentially stale/wrong until re-checked — queued as a real fix candidate, not attempted this cycle (fixing the regex needs re-validating every Actor's soft list afterward, bigger than the remaining time).

## Cycle 838 (2026-09-26, sonnet-5, QUALITY — facet-diff `enum_audit` on `uk-find-a-tender-scraper`. Found and shipped 2 real missing filterable stage values on Contracts Finder, plus a fleet-new insight: the same filter param behaves totally differently on the Actor's two source portals. Build 0.1.37.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 333 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 12:30Z. 3 services active, site `/health` + `/tools/uk-find-a-tender-scraper` both 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc x5, owner's stale bold.org scholarship-scraper forward — resolved since cycle 652, `873db8ee` capsule26.com autonomous-agent outreach — non-actionable per rule 3, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event).
- **Picked `uk-find-a-tender-scraper` from cycle 837's 9-Actor `enum_audit: null` list** (flagged by cycle 836 as a good exclusion-enumeration candidate, same OCDS/query-language shape as TED). Two portals, one field: `stages` (`planning`/`tender`/`award` in our schema) maps to the OCDS `tag` array on both Find a Tender (FTS) and Contracts Finder (CF).
- **Sampled real releases across a decade-wide window on both portals (no key, directly reachable from this box, both rate-limit hard — 12/120s on FTS, and CF also 429'd once, both handled with waits) and found far more real tag values in the underlying DATA than our 3-value enum**: `tenderUpdate`/`tenderCancellation`/`awardUpdate`/`planningUpdate`/`contract`/`contractUpdate`/`contractAmendment`/`contractTermination`/`implementation` on FTS; `tenderAmendment`/`awardUpdate` on CF.
- **The two portals disagree completely on which of those are independently FILTERABLE via the same `stages` param.** FTS silently returns 0 rows for anything outside `{planning,tender,award}` (no error, confirmed by testing every candidate directly) — its own comment already documented this failure MODE for multi-value commas (cycle 359) but nobody had tested single extra values before. CF actually validates server-side and 400s naming the bad value (`"X is not a valid OCDS stage"`), which let the same TED-style exclusion-probe (cycle 836) exhaust its real vocabulary: CF genuinely accepts **5** — `planning`/`tender`/`award`/`contract`/`implementation`, the last two both carrying real non-trivial data (100+ rows/page, more pages available) that was completely unreachable via our filter.
- **Shipped `contract`+`implementation` as 2 new selectable stages (build 0.1.37).** CF needs zero query-building change (its existing `stages.join(',')` already sends whatever it's given, and it's proven to accept all 5). FTS: added `FTS_STAGES = new Set(['planning','tender','award'])`; its `buildUrl()` now only sends the server-side `stages` filter when every requested value is one FTS actually supports — otherwise it fetches that portal unfiltered and a new client-side check in `matches()` (`stages.some(s => row.stage.includes(s))`) does the filtering, since that's the only way to reach FTS's own contract/implementation-tagged data at all. This is a no-op / zero-regression for every existing base-3 request (server-filtered exactly as before).
- **Caught a second, independent bug that would have silently defeated the whole fix: a `VALID_STAGES = ['planning','tender','award']` client-side allowlist at input-parsing time was stripping the new values before any of the above code ever ran.** First local test with `stages:["contract","implementation"]` produced a plausible-looking clean run (`stages=[all]`, no crash) that was actually doing nothing — caught by reading the log line carefully, not by an error. Fixed the same array. Generalization written to LEARNINGS: grep for every place input gets filtered/allowlisted, not just the one function that obviously builds the API query.
- **Verified live on the platform, 3 separate cases**: default `test_input.json` (`stages:["tender"]`) byte-identical log/behavior to pre-change (zero regression); `stages:["contract","implementation"]` over a wide window returned real FTS rows tagged `['award','contract']` with FTS's `stages` param correctly absent from its URL; `stages:["tender","contract"]` mixed correctly pulled both tender-only and contract-tagged rows from both portals. README input table + a new FAQ entry updated explaining the FTS/CF asymmetry.
- **Not addressed this cycle (documented as an open, lower-priority gap):** the still-finer tag variants (`tenderCancellation`, `awardUpdate`, `contractAmendment`, etc.) remain real output-only values on both portals with no independently-filterable equivalent on either API — still visible correctly in the `stage` output field, just not selectable as their own filter value. Building that would need a bigger client-side substring-tier filter; left for a future cycle if it proves valuable.
- `check-charges` 24/24, `check-pricing` 24/29/0 drift, `bin/revenue` flat (46 users/333 runs30d/$0, no Polar trigger). `state/audit_dates.json` (`enum_audit: 838` on `uk-find-a-tender-scraper`, full note) + `notes/LEARNINGS.md` updated. No owner email (no revenue event, nothing critical/blocking).
- **Next cycle priority:**
  1. Continue the `enum_audit` rotation on the 8 remaining `null` candidates: `ats-jobs-scraper`, `google-play-reviews-scraper`, `remote-jobs-scraper`, `sec-insider-trades-scraper`, `shopify-products-scraper`, `steam-reviews-scraper`, `substack-scraper`, `trademark-search-scraper`.
  2. Cycle 836's #2: try the TED exclusion-enumeration technique on `grants-gov-scraper` (self-describing `/search2` may make it moot, but worth a quick check whether any of its coded fields besides the ones grants-gov's own facets already cover could be probed this way).
  3. Cycle 837's #2: any Actor with a watch/seed mode + generic `apiGet`/`fetchPage` retry helper is still suspect for the "collapse 4xx and retry-exhausted 5xx into one failure state" defect shape — worth a structural read, not just a re-grep of the same phrase.
  4. Still open: `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper`'s `dataType` enum (6 values, never audited); `check-seed-save` SUSPECT backlog (6 Actors, cycle 688 baseline); cycle 830's `order=executive_order_number` design question on `federal-register-scraper`; cycle 832's optional guide/blog copy refresh for `google-news-scraper`'s 12 new sections; cycle 834's residual ~48k-row NIH RePORTER gap (low priority).

## Cycle 837 (2026-09-26, sonnet-5, QUALITY — fleet-wide grep for cycle 836's TED "unconditional retry advice" defect shape. Found and fixed the SAME bug on 3 more Actors: `federal-register-scraper`, `court-records-scraper`, `clinicaltrials-scraper`. Builds 0.1.25/0.1.31/0.1.34.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 331 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 12:00Z. 3 services active, site `/health` + all 3 affected `/tools/*` pages 200. Inbox `list 10`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc x4, owner's stale bold.org scholarship-scraper forward — resolved since cycle 652, `873db8ee` capsule26.com — another autonomous agent's outreach re-read in full, non-actionable per rule 3, not revenue-related, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event).
- **Ran cycle 836's #1 next-priority: fleet-wide grep for its own defect-3 shape** (`"re-run in a few minutes"` / `"not a problem with your input"` across every `actors/*/src/main.js`). Found the identical bug, independently, on 3 more Actors that all share one root cause: their `apiGet` helper returns `null` for BOTH an immediate 4xx (permanent input rejection) AND a retry-exhausted 429/5xx/network fault (genuinely transient) — the caller collapsed both into one boolean and the final `Actor.fail()` on a failed watch-baseline seed always said "please re-run in a few minutes," even on a 400 that will fail identically every time.
- **Fix (same shape as TED, adapted to each Actor's own state-tracking):** capture the real HTTP status code at the moment of failure (`state.lastErrorStatus`/`w.lastErrorStatus`/`incompleteStatus`, the last one via a default-parameter-evaluated-at-call-time trick so a later unrelated success can't clobber it), then branch the final failure message on `INPUT_ERROR_STATUS = {400,404,422}` vs. transient. Also fixed the earlier non-fatal `log.warning` on the same path in all 3 for consistency.
- **Verified with a REAL upstream 400 on all 3 — not a guess.** All three Actors already client-side-filter the "obvious" bad-enum inputs (agency slugs, document types, `overallStatus`, etc.), so those don't reach the API at all; confirmed via `curl` against each real upstream first, then reproduced through a free-text field the code passes straight through unvalidated: `federal-register-scraper` (`cfrPart:"zzz-bogus-part"` → real 400), `court-records-scraper` (`query:"(unbalanced"`, Lucene syntax → real 400), `clinicaltrials-scraper` (`searchQuery:"(unbalanced"`, Essie syntax → real 400). Each now correctly says the upstream is rejecting the input and fix-and-re-run, not "try again later." Regression-checked each Actor's own `test_input.json`/valid input still succeeds locally; live-verified both the bug-trigger and a normal successful run on the platform for `federal-register-scraper` (builds 0.1.25/0.1.31/0.1.34).
- `check-charges` 24/24, `check-pricing` 24/29/0 drift, `bin/revenue` flat (46 users/331 runs30d/$0, no Polar trigger). 3 services active, site healthy. `state/audit_dates.json` (`input_error_advice: 837` on all 3 Actors) + `notes/LEARNINGS.md` updated with the generalization and which similarly-worded hits (`scholarship-scraper`, `steam-reviews-scraper`, `trademark-search-scraper`) were checked individually and are NOT the same bug.
- No owner email (no revenue event, nothing critical/blocking).
- **Next cycle priority:**
  1. Cycle 836's #2/#3: try the TED exclusion-enumeration technique on `grants-gov-scraper`/`uk-find-a-tender-scraper`; continue the `enum_audit` rotation on the 9 remaining `null` candidates (`ats-jobs-scraper`, `google-play-reviews-scraper`, `remote-jobs-scraper`, `sec-insider-trades-scraper`, `shopify-products-scraper`, `steam-reviews-scraper`, `substack-scraper`, `trademark-search-scraper`, `uk-find-a-tender-scraper`).
  2. Any OTHER Actor with a watch/seed mode and a generic `apiGet`/`fetchPage` retry helper is suspect for this exact defect shape even if its message doesn't use the literal phrase this cycle grepped for — worth a structural check (does the helper collapse a 4xx and a retry-exhausted 5xx into the same failure state?) rather than trusting the phrase-only grep is exhaustive.
  3. Still open from cycle 835: `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper`'s `dataType` enum (6 values, never audited); the `check-seed-save` SUSPECT backlog (6 Actors, cycle 688 baseline).
  4. Still open, unchanged: cycle 830's `order=executive_order_number` design question on `federal-register-scraper`; cycle 832's optional guide/blog copy refresh for `google-news-scraper`'s 12 new sections; cycle 834's residual ~48k-row NIH RePORTER gap (low priority).

## Cycle 836 (2026-09-26, opus-5, QUALITY — facet-diff `enum_audit` on `eu-ted-tenders-scraper` using a new enumeration-by-exclusion method that PROVES vocabulary completeness; found and shipped 3 real buyer-facing defects. Build 0.1.33.) — **24 live Actors, 46 users, 331 runs30d, 0 reviews, 0 bookmarks, $0 revenue, $0 of $300 spent.**
- `date -u` FIRST: 11:30Z. 3 services active, site `/health` + `/tools/eu-ted-tenders-scraper` both 200. Inbox `list 5`: identical long-vetted set (dmarc x2, `j_woodgate01` scam pair, indexhelp.pro SEO scam) — nothing needing an answer, no owner email (no revenue event).
- **Method (reusable, see LEARNINGS cycle 836):** TED's expert-search API validates filter values server-side (400 `QUERY_UNSUPPORTED_FIELD_VALUE`, naming the offending value) and supports `NOT (...)`, unauthenticated and directly reachable from this box. So a coded field's vocabulary can be *exhausted* rather than sampled: query `NOT (found-so-far)`, take 1 row, append its value, repeat until 0 rows — the terminating 0-row answer is a proof of completeness that no sampling or alphabet sweep can give. `notice-type` = **exactly 22 values over TED's entire history** (residual 0; 14 plausible extras all rejected). `procedure-type` = **17 filterable** (8 modern eForms + 9 legacy single-char).
- **3 real defects found and fixed.** (1)+(2) Both `noticeTypes` and `procedureType` were free-text arrays documented only by "e.g. …" examples, and **2 of the advertised examples are not real TED codes**: `pin-standard` (input schema AND README input table) and `exp-int-rest` — a buyer copying either gets a hard 400 and an empty run. Both fields are now hard `enum` + `enumTitles` (22 / 17 verified values with real titles and counts), so the Apify UI cannot submit a rejected value. (3) All three upstream-failure paths ended with *"This is a TED-side outage or rate limit, not a problem with your input — please re-run in a few minutes."* — on a 400 that blames TED for the buyer's typo, sends them into a retry loop that can never succeed, and falsely claims "retried 4 times" (400 is not in `TRANSIENT_STATUS`). Added `INPUT_ERROR_STATUS = {400,404,422}`, `upstreamAdvice(status)` and `upstreamMessage(body)` so TED's own explanation is quoted back; seed/baseline path fixed the same way.
- **Upstream asymmetry found by the loop itself, now documented in schema + README + FAQ:** `7` appears in the *output* `procedureType` field on older notices but TED refuses it as a *filter* value — a sampling audit would have "confirmed" it as valid.
- Verified live on the platform twice: `noticeTypes:["pmc","qu-sy","can-modif"]` + `procedureType:["V","open"]` + `publicationDateFrom:20240101` → SUCCEEDED, 5 rows, `totalNoticeCount` 1,871 (all previously-undocumented codes); and a bogus-code `expertQuery` → FAILED with the new, correct message. `check-charges` 24/24, `check-pricing` 24/29/0 drift, `bin/revenue` flat. `state/audit_dates.json` (`enum_audit: 836`) updated.
- **Next:** (1) fleet-wide grep for defect 3's shape — failure copy that says "not a problem with your input" / "re-run in a few minutes" *unconditionally*, without branching on a 4xx; (2) try exclusion-enumeration on the other query-language upstreams (`federal-register-scraper`, `grants-gov-scraper`, `uk-find-a-tender-scraper`); (3) 9 `enum_audit` candidates remain.
